\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\rho^{-2s-2\sigma}c_{\rho}^{2}(\sigma).

For high high term for the cubic terms after Bony decomposition, we can deal it in the same manner as (
3.25
), by noting that
\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\mu^{d-\frac{7}{2}}\lesssim\rho^{d-\frac{7}{2}}
, if
d\geq 4
.

Combining the four
contributions proves (
3.26
).

∎

Remark 3.7
.

In our framework, multiplying the nonlinear term by
u_{j}
has the same effect as taking one derivative. Taking the nonlinear term
u_{i}u_{j}u_{l}
for example, we have the decomposition

\displaystyle u_{i}u_{j}u_{l}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{3}}P_{\mu_{1}}u_{i}P_{\mu_{2}}u_{j}P_{\mu_{3}}u_{l}.

Without loss of generality, we assume
\mu_{1}\leq\mu_{2}\leq\mu_{3},
and define
\|f\|_{X}
as some norm of
f
, then schematically, it holds

\displaystyle\left\|P_{\mu_{1}}u_{i}P_{\mu_{2}}u_{j}P_{\mu_{3}}u_{l}\right\|_{X}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|P_{\mu_{1}}u_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\left\|P_{\mu_{2}}u_{j}P_{\mu_{3}}u_{l}\right\|_{X}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|P_{\mu_{1}}u_{i}\|_{\dot{B}^{s+1}_{2,1}}\left\|P_{\mu_{2}}u_{j}P_{\mu_{3}}u_{l}\right\|_{X}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{8}}\mu_{2}\left\|P_{\mu_{2}}u_{j}P_{\mu_{3}}u_{l}\right\|_{X},

where we used the fact
\|P_{\mu_{1}}u_{i}\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{8}}.

Thus cubic terms are more favorable than differentiated quadratic terms. We shall therefore suppress cubic
contributions in later arguments whenever they are covered by the preceding
principle and focus on the more delicate quadratic interactions.

The following two lemmas are devoted to estimating the typical nonlinear terms involving the combination of the Schrödinger solution
\psi
and elliptic solution
u_{\mathfrak{T}}
for
\mathfrak{T}\in\{3,4,5\}.

The main obstacle in Lemma
3.8
is that the low frequency factor

P_{\ll\rho}\mathbf{U}
is not directly controlled by the bootstrap bounds.
We therefore decompose it dyadically and use the elliptic equation at each
frequency, thereby converting the quadratic interaction into cubic terms with
additional elliptic gain.

Lemma 3.8
.

For
\mathbf{U}=(W,u_{3})
and
W=V-2A
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}L(P_{\ll\rho}\mathbf{U},\psi_{\sim\rho})\cdot\partial_{x}\overline{\psi}_{\rho}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\right|\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\rho^{-2s-2\sigma}c^{2}_{\rho}(\sigma).

(3.28)

Proof.

By decomposition (
2.1
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}L(P_{\ll\rho}\mathbf{U},\psi_{\sim\rho})\cdot\partial_{x}\overline{\psi}_{\rho}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\right|

(3.29)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}=1}^{d}\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}L(P_{\ll\rho}\mathbf{U},\psi_{\sim\rho,\gamma})\cdot\partial_{x}\overline{\psi}_{\rho,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\right|.

By symmetry, it suffices to consider the case
\gamma=\gamma^{\prime}=1
.

Recall equation (
2.35
) for
\mathbf{U}
, namely

\displaystyle\Delta\mathbf{U}=h\cdot\partial_{x}^{2}\mathbf{U}+\partial_{x}h\cdot\partial_{x}u_{\mathfrak{T}}+\partial_{x}(\lambda\wedge\lambda)+u_{i}u_{j}u_{l},

where
u_{3}=\partial_{x}h,\mathfrak{T}\in\{3,4,5\}
and
i,j,l\in\{1,2,3,4,5\}
.
Applying
\Delta^{-1}P_{\mu}
to the above equation and using the Fourier transform, Bony’s decomposition, and the formula (
2.5
)–(
2.7
) for
L
notation, we obtain

\displaystyle P_{\mu}\mathbf{U}=

\displaystyle L(P_{\leq\mu}h,P_{\mu}\mathbf{U})+\mu^{-1}L(P_{\mu}u_{3},P_{\ll\mu}u_{\mathfrak{T}})

(3.30)

\displaystyle+\mu^{-2}\sum_{\rho\gg\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\mu}P_{\mu}L(P_{\nu}u_{3},\partial_{x}P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{\mathfrak{T}})

\displaystyle+\mu^{-2}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}P_{\mu}L(P_{\nu}u_{3},\partial_{x}P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{\mathfrak{T}})

\displaystyle+\mu^{-1}P_{\mu}(\lambda\wedge\lambda)+\mu^{-2}P_{\mu}(u_{i}u_{j}u_{l}).

Consequently,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}L(P_{\ll\rho}\mathbf{U},\psi_{\sim\rho,1})\cdot\partial_{x}\overline{\psi}_{\rho,1}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\rho\sup_{\tau}\big\|\left\|P_{\mu}\mathbf{U}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\psi_{\sim\rho,1}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|\psi_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\big\|_{L^{1}_{t,y}}

(3.31)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\rho\|P_{\leq\mu}h\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\cdot\sup_{\tau}\big\|\left\|P_{\mu}\mathbf{U}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\psi_{\sim\rho,1}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|\psi_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\big\|_{L^{1}_{t,y}}

(3.32)

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\rho\mu^{-1}\sup_{\tau}\big\|\left\|L(P_{\mu}u_{3},P_{\ll\mu}u_{\mathfrak{T}})\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\psi_{\sim\rho,1}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|\psi_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\big\|_{L^{1}_{t,y}}

(3.33)

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\rho\gg\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\mu}\rho\mu^{-2}\sup_{\tau}\big\|\left\|P_{\mu}L(P_{\nu}u_{3},P_{\nu^{\prime}}\partial_{x}u_{\mathfrak{T}})\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}

(3.34)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\cdot\|\psi_{\sim\rho,1}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|\psi_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\big\|_{L^{1}_{t,y}}

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\rho\mu^{-2}\sup_{\tau}\big\|\left\|P_{\mu}L(P_{\nu}u_{3},P_{\nu^{\prime}}\partial_{x}u_{\mathfrak{T}})\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}

(3.35)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\cdot\left\|\psi_{\sim\rho,1}\right\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\left\|\psi_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\right\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\big\|_{L^{1}_{t,y}}

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\rho\mu^{-1}\sup_{\tau}\big\|\left\|P_{\mu}(\lambda\wedge\lambda)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\cdot\left\|\psi_{\sim\rho}\right\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\left\|\psi_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\right\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\big\|_{L^{1}_{t,y}}

(3.36)

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\rho\mu^{-2}\sup_{\tau}\big\|\left\|P_{\mu}(u_{i}u_{j}u_{k})\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\cdot\|\psi_{\sim\rho,1}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|\psi_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\big\|_{L^{1}_{t,y}},

(3.37)

where we let
y_{1}=x_{1}
in
y=(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3})
.
It is obvious that (
3.32
) can be absorbed by (
3.31
), since by (
3.2
) we have
\|P_{\leq\mu}h\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{8}}
.
(
3.36
) has been proved in Lemma
3.5
and (
3.33
) is a special case of (
3.12
), while (
3.37
) can been handled in the way as in Remark
3.7
.
Hence we are left with the estimates of (
3.33
), (
3.34
) and (
3.35
).

Observing that the decomposition (
2.1
) and Bernstein’s inequality give

\displaystyle\eqref{J11t3_0}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\rho\gg\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\mu}\rho\mu^{d-3}\sup_{\tau}\big\|\left\|L(P_{\nu}u_{3},P_{\nu^{\prime}}\partial_{x}u_{\mathfrak{T}})\right\|_{L^{1}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}

\displaystyle\cdot\|\psi_{\sim\rho,1}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\|\psi_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\big\|_{L^{1}_{t,x_{1}}},

we can see (
3.34
) can be estimated just as (
3.33
).

For (
3.35
), we can use (
1.27
), (
1.28
) and (
1.30
) to obtain

\displaystyle\eqref{J11t3}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\rho\mu^{-2}\left\|P_{\mu}(L(P_{\nu}u_{3},P_{\nu^{\prime}}\partial_{x}u_{\mathfrak{T}}))\right\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\cdot\big\|\|\psi_{\sim\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\left\|\psi_{\rho}\right\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}_{y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\rho\mu^{d-2}\nu^{\prime}\left\|P_{\nu}u_{3}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\left\|P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{\mathfrak{T}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\cdot\left\|\psi_{\sim\rho}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\left\|\psi_{\rho}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\sum_{\rho^{\prime}\sim\rho}\rho\mu^{d-2}\nu^{\prime}\nu^{-s-1}c_{\nu}(0)(\nu^{\prime})^{-s-1}c_{\nu^{\prime}}(0)

\displaystyle\cdot(\rho^{\prime})^{-s-\sigma}c_{\rho^{\prime}}(\sigma)\rho^{-s-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\rho^{-2s-2\sigma}c_{\rho}^{2}(\sigma).

This completes the proof.
∎

Different from Lemma
3.8
, the estimates in Lemma
3.9
can be controlled directly by
bootstrap assumption of
u_{\mathfrak{T}}
.

Lemma 3.9
.

For
\mathfrak{T}\in\{3,4,5\}
, we have

\displaystyle\|L(P_{\sim\rho}u_{\mathfrak{T}},\partial_{x}\psi_{\ll\rho})\cdot\overline{\psi}_{\rho}\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{8}}\rho^{-2s-2\sigma}c^{2}_{\rho}(\sigma),

(3.38)

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu^{\prime}\sim\nu\gtrsim\rho}\|L(P_{\nu}u_{\mathfrak{T}},\psi_{\nu^{\prime}})\cdot\partial_{x}\overline{\psi}_{\rho}\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{8}}\rho^{-2s-2\sigma}c^{2}_{\rho}(\sigma).

(3.39)

Proof.

The estimates (
3.38
) and (
3.39
) follows from (
3.8
) and (
3.7
) respectively. To be specific, we can use (
1.28
), (
1.30
), (
3.8
) and Hölder’s inequality to obtain

\displaystyle\,\left\|L(P_{\sim\rho}u_{\mathfrak{T}},\partial_{x}\psi_{\ll\rho})\cdot\overline{\psi}_{\rho}\right\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left\|P_{\sim\rho}u_{\mathfrak{T}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\left\|P_{\ll\rho}\partial_{x}\psi\right\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{\infty}_{z}}\left\|\psi_{\rho}\right\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sum_{\rho^{\prime}\sim\rho}(\rho^{\prime})^{-s-1-\sigma}c_{\rho^{\prime}}(\sigma)\cdot\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{8}}\rho^{\frac{3}{4}}\cdot\rho^{-s+\frac{1}{4}-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{8}}\rho^{-2s-2\sigma}c^{2}_{\rho}(\sigma)

and by (
3.7
) and the slow variation property of the
\sigma
-frequency envelope (
1.26
)

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu^{\prime}\sim\nu\gtrsim\rho}\left\|L(P_{\nu}u_{\mathfrak{T}},\psi_{\nu^{\prime}})\cdot\partial_{x}\overline{\psi}_{\rho}\right\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\nu^{\prime}\sim\nu\gtrsim\rho}\rho\left\|P_{\nu}u_{\mathfrak{T}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\left\|\psi_{\nu^{\prime}}\right\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}\|\psi_{\rho}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{\infty}_{z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sum_{\nu^{\prime}\sim\nu\gtrsim\rho}\rho\nu^{-s-1-\sigma}c_{\nu}(\sigma)\cdot(\nu^{\prime})^{-s+\frac{1}{4}}c_{\nu^{\prime}}(0)\cdot\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}\rho^{-\frac{1}{4}}c_{\rho}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{8}}\rho^{-2s-2\sigma}c^{2}_{\rho}(\sigma).

∎

We next establish an
L_{x}^{2}
estimate for the typical term
u_{i}u_{j}
, which will be used in the elliptic estimates.

Lemma 3.10
.

For
i,j=1,2,3,4,5
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|P_{\rho}(u_{i}u_{j})\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{4}}\rho^{-s+1-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma).

(3.40)

Proof.

By the Bony’s decomposition in (
2.4
), it suffices to estimate the low–high and high–high contributions, since
u_{i}u_{j}
is symmetry.
By Hölder’s inequality, Bernstein’s inequality (
3.2
) and (
1.27
), we see that

\displaystyle\|P_{\ll\rho}u_{i}P_{\rho}u_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|P_{\ll\rho}u_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\cdot\|P_{\rho}u_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{8}}\cdot\rho\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}\rho^{-s-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{4}}\rho^{-s+1-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma)

and

\displaystyle\Big\|P_{\rho}\big(\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}P_{\nu}u_{i}P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{j}\big)\Big\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\rho^{\frac{d}{2}}\|P_{\nu}u_{i}P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\rho^{\frac{d}{2}}\|P_{\nu}u_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\rho^{\frac{d}{2}}\nu^{-s}c_{\nu}(0)\cdot(\nu^{\prime})^{-s-\sigma}c_{\nu^{\prime}}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{4}}\ \rho^{-s+1-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma),

where we have used (
1.26
).
Combining the preceding bounds proves (
3.40
).
∎

The next lemma treats the nonlinear terms involving
B
.

Lemma 3.11
.

For
i,j\in\{1,2,3,4,5\},
we have

\displaystyle\rho^{-1}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}L(\partial_{x}B_{\ll\rho},P_{\sim\rho}u_{i})\cdot P_{\rho}u_{j}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{8}}\rho^{-2s-2\sigma}c_{\rho}^{2}(\sigma),

(3.41)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}L(B_{\sim\rho},P_{\ll\rho}u_{i})\cdot P_{\rho}u_{j}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{8}}\rho^{-2s-2\sigma}c_{\rho}^{2}(\sigma),

(3.42)

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}P_{\rho}L(B_{\nu},P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{i})\cdot P_{\rho}u_{j}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{8}}\rho^{-2s-2\sigma}c_{\rho}^{2}(\sigma).

(3.43)

Proof.

The (
3.41
) follows from
Hölder’s inequality, (
1.30
) and (
3.6
) directly:

\displaystyle\rho^{-1}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}L(\partial_{x}B_{\ll\rho},P_{\sim\rho}u_{i})\cdot P_{\rho}u_{j}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\rho^{-1}\left\|\partial_{x}B_{\ll\rho}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,y}L^{\infty}_{z}}\left\|P_{\sim\rho}u_{i}\right\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}\left\|P_{\rho}u_{j}\right\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{8}}\rho^{-2s-2\sigma}c_{\rho}^{2}(\sigma).

Similarly for (
3.43
),

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}P_{\rho}L(B_{\nu},P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{i})\cdot P_{\rho}u_{j}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\left\|B_{\nu}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\left\|P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{i}\right\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}\left\|P_{\rho}u_{j}\right\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{\infty}_{z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{-\frac{3}{8}}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\nu^{-s}c_{\nu}(0)(\nu^{\prime})^{-s+\frac{1}{4}-\sigma}c_{\nu^{\prime}}(\sigma)\rho^{-s+\frac{1}{4}-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{8}}\rho^{-2s-2\sigma}c_{\rho}^{2}(\sigma),

where we have used (
3.7
) and (
1.26
).
Turning to (
3.42
), it follows from the decomposition (
2.1
), and (
3.11
) that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}L(B_{\sim\rho},P_{\ll\rho}u_{i})\cdot P_{\rho}u_{j}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|P_{\sim\rho}B\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\cdot\sum_{\gamma=1}^{d}\left\|\|P_{\ll\rho}u_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\widehat{x}_{\gamma}}}\|P_{\rho,\gamma}u_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{\gamma}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{8}}\rho^{-2s-2\sigma}c_{\rho}^{2}(\sigma).

This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

4.
Energy estimate

In this section, we are going to establish the energy estimate for the nonlinear Schrödinger equation (
1.16
) coupled with the elliptic system (
1.17
) and (
1.18
). Letting
Q_{\rho}=P_{\rho}
in the equation (
2.36
) implies

\displaystyle\sqrt{-1}\partial_{t}\psi_{\rho}+\partial_{\alpha}(g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\psi_{\rho})=P_{\rho}\mathcal{N}-\partial_{\alpha}([P_{\rho},h^{\alpha\beta}]\partial_{\beta}\psi):=\mathcal{I}_{\rho},

(4.1)

multiplying which with
\overline{\psi}_{\rho}
, taking the imaginary part and then integrating over
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
yield

\displaystyle\|\psi_{\rho}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}-\|P_{\rho}\psi_{0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\operatorname{Im}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\overline{\psi}_{\rho}\cdot P_{\rho}\mathcal{N}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

(4.2)

\displaystyle\,-\operatorname{Im}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{\alpha}([P_{\rho},h^{\alpha\beta}]\partial_{\beta}\psi)\cdot\overline{\psi}_{\rho}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

(4.3)

For the second nonlinear term (
4.3
), we can use integration by parts and Bony’s decomposition of commutator (
2.11
) to obtain

\displaystyle-\mathrm{Im}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{\alpha}([P_{\rho},h^{\alpha\beta}]\partial_{\beta}\psi)\cdot\overline{\psi}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\mathrm{Im}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}([P_{\rho},h^{\alpha\beta}]\partial_{\beta}\psi)\partial_{\alpha}\overline{\psi}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}L(\partial_{x}P_{\ll\rho}h^{\alpha\beta},\psi_{\sim\rho})\cdot\partial_{\alpha}\overline{\psi}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

(4.4)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}L(\partial_{\beta}P_{\ll\rho}\psi,P_{\sim\rho}h^{\alpha\beta})\cdot\partial_{\alpha}\overline{\psi}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

(4.5)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\nu^{\prime}\sim\nu\gtrsim\rho}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}P_{\rho}L(P_{\nu}h^{\alpha\beta},\partial_{\beta}\psi_{\nu^{\prime}})\cdot\partial_{\alpha}\overline{\psi}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

(4.6)

Given that
h^{\alpha\beta}=g^{\alpha\beta}-I_{d}=(I_{d}+h_{\alpha\beta})^{-1}-I_{d}=-h_{\alpha\beta}+o((h_{\alpha\beta})^{2})
and
h_{\alpha\beta}
satisfies (
2.22
), then the estimate of (
4.4
) is completely similar to that in Lemma
3.8
. Meanwhile, the estimates for (
4.5
) and (
4.6
) follow from Lemma
3.9
for
u_{\mathfrak{T}}=\partial_{x}h.

For the first nonlinear term (
4.2
), it follows from (
2.38
) that

\displaystyle\eqref{enenltn}=

\displaystyle\,\mathrm{Im}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}P_{\rho}\left(\mathbf{U}\cdot\partial_{x}\psi\right)\overline{\psi}_{\rho}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

(4.7)

\displaystyle\,+\mathrm{Im}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}P_{\rho}\left(\psi u_{i}u_{j}\right)\overline{\psi}_{\rho}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

(4.8)

\displaystyle\,+\mathrm{Im}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}[P_{\rho},B]\psi\cdot\overline{\psi}_{\rho}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

(4.9)

(
4.7
) can be estimated exactly the same as (
4.3
), while the estimate of (
4.8
) is similar to (
4.7
) by noting Remark
3.7
.
Thus, it suffices to estimate
(
4.9
) and one can use (
2.11
) to obtain

\displaystyle\eqref{enet3}=

\displaystyle\,\rho^{-1}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}L(\partial_{x}B_{\ll\rho},\psi_{\sim\rho})\cdot\overline{\psi}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

(4.10)

\displaystyle\,+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}L(B_{\sim\rho},\psi_{\ll\rho})\cdot\overline{\psi}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

(4.11)

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}P_{\rho}L(B_{\nu},\psi_{\nu^{\prime}})\cdot\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

(4.12)

which have been estimated by (
3.41
)–(
3.43
) with the choice
u_{i}=u_{j}=\psi
.

The combination of estimates for (
4.10
)–(
4.12
) implies

\displaystyle\eqref{enet3}\lesssim\rho^{-2s-2\sigma}c_{\rho}^{2}(\sigma).

(4.13)

Together with the estimates for (
4.7
), (
4.8
), and (
4.3
), this implies (
1.32
) for
i=1
.

5.
Bilinear estimate

This section is devoted to establishing the bilinear estimate (
1.34
). Without loss of generality, we may always set
\gamma=1
and
\tau=0.

Replacing
P_{\rho}
in (
4.1
) with
P_{\mu}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sqrt{-1}\partial_{t}\psi_{\mu}+\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{d}\partial_{\alpha}(g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\psi_{\mu})=P_{\mu}\mathcal{N}-\partial_{\alpha}([P_{\mu},h^{\alpha\beta}]\partial_{\beta}\psi):=\mathcal{I}_{\mu},

(5.1)

Similarly,
setting
Q_{\rho}=P_{\rho,1}
in (
2.36
) and
\phi_{\rho}=\psi_{\rho,1}
gives

\displaystyle\sqrt{-1}\partial_{t}\phi_{\rho}+\partial_{\alpha}(g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\phi_{\rho})=P_{\rho,1}\mathcal{N}-\partial_{\alpha}([P_{\rho,1},h^{\alpha\beta}]\partial_{\beta}\psi):=\mathcal{G}_{\rho}.

(5.2)

Following
[
31
]
, we can derive the momentum balance law for (
5.2
) as

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Im}\left(\phi_{\rho}\partial_{1}\overline{\phi}_{\rho}\right)\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}

(5.3)

\displaystyle+\partial_{1}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}2\mathrm{Re}(\partial_{1}\phi_{\rho}\,g^{1\beta}\partial_{\beta}\overline{\phi}_{\rho})-\partial_{1}\mathrm{Re}(\phi_{\rho}\,g^{1\beta}\partial_{\beta}\overline{\phi}_{\rho})\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Re}(\partial_{\alpha}\phi_{\rho}\partial_{1}h^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\overline{\phi}_{\rho})\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Re}(\mathcal{G}_{\rho}\partial_{1}\overline{\phi}_{\rho})-\mathrm{Re}(\overline{\phi}_{\rho}\partial_{1}\mathcal{G}_{\rho})\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1},

while the mass balance law for (
5.1
) is given by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\left|P_{\mu}\psi\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}+\partial_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Im}(\overline{\psi}_{\mu}g^{1\beta}\partial_{\beta}\psi_{\mu})\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}

(5.4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{I}_{\mu}\overline{\psi_{\mu}})\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}.

Employing the div-curl Lemma
2.2
for system (
5.3
) and (
5.4
) yields

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}+\mathrm{II}+\mathrm{III}

(5.5)

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho\max_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\phi_{\rho}(t,\cdot)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\max_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\psi_{\mu}(t,\cdot)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}

(5.6)

\displaystyle\,+\Big|\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\left(\int_{-\infty}^{x_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Im}\left(\phi_{\rho}\partial_{1}\overline{\phi}_{\rho}\right)\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}y\right)

(5.7)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Im}\left(\overline{\psi}_{\mu}\mathcal{I}_{\mu}\right)\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\,+\Big|\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\left(\int^{+\infty}_{x_{1}}\left\|\psi_{\mu}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\mathrm{d}y\right)

(5.8)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Re}\left(\partial_{\alpha}\phi_{\rho}\partial_{1}h^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\overline{\phi}_{\rho}\right)\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\,+\Big|\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\left(\int^{+\infty}_{x_{1}}\left\|\psi_{\mu}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\mathrm{d}y\right)\Big|

(5.9)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Re}\left(\overline{\phi}_{\rho}\partial_{1}\mathcal{G}_{\rho}-\mathcal{G}_{\rho}\partial_{1}\overline{\phi}_{\rho}\right)\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t\Big|,

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\psi_{\mu}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Re}(\partial_{1}\phi_{\rho}g^{1\beta}\partial_{\beta}\overline{\phi}_{\rho})\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Re}(\psi_{\mu}\partial_{1}\overline{\psi}_{\mu})\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Re}(\phi_{\rho}\,g^{1\beta}\partial_{\beta}\overline{\phi}_{\rho})\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}

\displaystyle=-\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Im}(\phi_{\rho}\partial_{1}\overline{\phi}_{\rho})\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Im}(\overline{\psi}_{\mu}g^{1\beta}\partial_{\beta}\psi_{\mu})\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t.

Noting
g^{\alpha\beta}=h^{\alpha\beta}+\delta_{\alpha\beta}
, we can use (
2.9
) to obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}=

\displaystyle\,\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\psi_{\mu}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\partial_{1}\phi_{\rho}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\,+\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\psi_{\mu}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Re}(\partial_{1}\phi_{\rho}h^{1\beta}\partial_{\beta}\overline{\phi}_{\rho})\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle\,\rho^{2}\sup_{\tau}\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\psi_{\mu}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\phi_{\rho}^{\tau}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle-C\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{8}}\rho^{2}\sup_{\tau}\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\psi_{\mu}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\phi_{\rho}^{\tau}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle\,\rho^{2}\sup_{\tau}\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\psi_{\mu}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\phi_{\rho}^{\tau}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t

by taking
L^{\infty}
norm of
h^{1\beta}
and using (
3.2
).

Similarly, for
\mathrm{II}
and
\mathrm{III}
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}+\mathrm{III}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\rho\mu\sup_{\tau}\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\psi_{\mu}^{\tau}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\phi_{\rho}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t

where
\|g^{\alpha\beta}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1.
By the assumption
\mu\ll\rho
, we finally obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}+\mathrm{II}+\mathrm{III}\gtrsim\rho^{2}\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\left|\psi_{\mu}\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\phi_{\rho}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t.

(5.10)

We now come to the estimates of the nonlinear terms (
5.7
)–(
5.9
).
For (
5.7
), we have

\displaystyle\eqref{biNt1}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left\|\phi_{\rho}\partial_{1}\overline{\phi}_{\rho}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}_{x}}\left\|\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{I}_{\mu}\overline{\psi}_{\mu}\right)\right\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\rho\left\|\phi_{\rho}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\left\|\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{I}_{\mu}\overline{\psi}_{\mu}\right)\right\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\mu^{-2s}c_{\mu}^{2}(0)\rho^{-2s+1-2\sigma}c_{\rho}^{2}(\sigma),

where

\left\|\phi_{\rho}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\lesssim\left\|\psi_{\rho}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}

and we have established in Section
4
that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{I}_{\mu}\overline{\psi}_{\mu}\right)\right\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{8}}\mu^{-2s}c_{\mu}^{2}(0).

Regarding (
5.8
), the frequency of
\partial_{1}h^{\alpha\beta}
in (
5.8
) are less than the sum of those for
\phi_{\rho},\overline{\phi}_{\rho},\psi_{\mu}.
Hence, we can rewrite and estimate (
5.8
) as follows:

\displaystyle\eqref{non.bit1}=

\displaystyle\,\Big|\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\left(\int^{+\infty}_{x_{1}}\left\|\psi_{\mu}\right\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\mathrm{d}y\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Re}\left(\partial_{\alpha}\phi_{\rho}\partial_{1}P_{\lesssim\rho}h^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\overline{\phi}_{\rho}\right)\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left\|\psi_{\mu}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\cdot\|\partial_{\alpha}\phi_{\rho}P_{\lesssim\rho}\partial_{1}h^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\overline{\phi}_{\rho}\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}.

Then (
5.8
) can be estimated in exactly the same way as (
3.28
) and (
3.39
).

Moving to (
5.9
), we use (
2.41
) to obtain

\displaystyle\eqref{non.bit2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left\|\psi_{\mu}\right\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\cdot\Big(\rho\,\|L(\phi_{\rho},P_{\rho,1}(\mathbf{U}\cdot\partial_{x}\psi+\psi u_{i}u_{j})+[P_{\rho,1},B]\psi)\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+\|\partial_{x}\phi_{\rho}\cdot\partial_{\alpha}\big([P_{\rho,1},h^{\alpha\beta}]\partial_{\beta}\psi\big)\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}\Big)

(5.11)

\displaystyle\,+\Big|\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\left(\int^{+\infty}_{x_{1}}\left\|\psi_{\mu}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\mathrm{d}y\right)

(5.12)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\partial_{1}([P_{\rho,1},B]\psi)-\partial_{1}B\cdot|\phi_{\rho}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\,\mathrm{d}t\Big|.

For (
5.11
), by (
2.11
) and the same approach as in Section
4
, we have

\displaystyle\|L\big(\phi_{\rho},P_{\rho,1}(\mathbf{U}\cdot\partial_{x}\psi+\psi u_{i}u_{j})+[P_{\rho,1},B]\psi+\partial_{\alpha}([P_{\rho,1},h^{\alpha\beta}]\partial_{\beta}\psi)\big)\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{8}}\rho^{-2s-2\sigma}c_{\rho}^{2}(\sigma),

which gives

\displaystyle\eqref{non.bit2_1}\lesssim\mu^{-2s}c_{\mu}^{2}(0)\rho^{-2s+1-2\sigma}c_{\rho}^{2}(\sigma).

Turning to
(
5.12
)
, we shall do integration by part for the first term and it is sufficient to bound

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\left(\int^{+\infty}_{x_{1}}\left\|\psi_{\mu}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\mathrm{d}y\right)\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\partial_{1}B_{\lesssim\rho}\cdot|\phi_{\rho}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\,\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t\Big|

(5.13)

\displaystyle\,+\Big|\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\|\psi_{\mu}\|_{L^{2}_{\hat{x}_{1}}}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\phi_{\rho}[P_{\rho,1},B]\psi\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\,\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t\Big|,

(5.14)

where we have used the fact that
the frequencies of
B
in (
5.13
) are less than the sum of those for
\phi_{\rho},\overline{\phi}_{\rho},\psi_{\mu}
.

The estimate of (
5.13
) is analogous to that of (
4.10
),
since

\sup_{x_{1}}\int_{x_{1}}^{+\infty}\|\psi_{\mu}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}^{2}\,dy\leq\|\psi_{\mu}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}.

The estimate of (
5.14
) is analogous to that of (
4.9
),
after taking the
L^{\infty}_{x_{1}}
norm of

\|\psi_{\mu}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}
.
Combining these estimates implies

\displaystyle\eqref{non.bit2_4}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\mu^{-2s}\rho^{-2s+1-2\sigma}c_{\mu}^{2}(0)c_{\rho}^{2}(\sigma),

which closes the estimates of (
5.9
).

Since
(
5.6
)
can be bounded directly by energy estimates,
combining (
5.10
) and the estimates of (
5.5
), (
5.7
)–(
5.9
), we finally get

\displaystyle\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\psi_{\mu}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\phi_{\rho}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\,\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mu^{-2s}\rho^{-2s-2\sigma-1}c_{\mu}^{2}(0)c_{\rho}^{2}(\sigma),

which is the bilinear estimate (
1.34
) for
i,j=1.

6.
Interaction Morawetz estimate

We now establish the interaction Morawetz estimate (
1.35
) for

i=1
. Without loss of generality, we set
y_{J}=(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})
and
z_{J}=(x_{4},\cdots,x_{d})
in (
1.35
).

From (
4.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sqrt{-1}\partial_{t}\psi_{\rho}+\partial_{\alpha}(g_{\ll\rho}^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\psi_{\rho})=\mathcal{I}_{\rho}-\partial_{\alpha}(P_{\gtrsim\rho}h^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\psi_{\rho}):=\mathcal{I}_{\rho}^{h},

(6.1)

where using (
1.19
) we have

P_{\ll\rho}\partial_{t}g_{\alpha\beta}=P_{\ll\rho}(\partial_{x}u_{4}+u_{i}u_{j}).

(6.2)

Inheriting the notations from the Appendix, we apply Theorem
A.1
to (
6.1
) and derive the interaction Morawetz estimate:

\displaystyle\|\psi_{\rho}\|^{4}_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}

(6.3)

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho\|\psi_{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}(\|\psi_{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\|\operatorname{Im}(\overline{\psi}_{\rho}\mathcal{I}_{\rho}^{h})\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}_{x}})

(6.4)

\displaystyle\quad+\rho\|\psi_{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}(\|L(\psi_{\rho},\psi_{\rho},P_{\ll\rho}\partial_{t}g),\ L(\partial_{x}h_{\lesssim\rho},\psi_{\rho},\partial_{x}\psi_{\rho})\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}})

(6.5)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\Big|\int a_{k}(y-y^{\prime})\,|\psi_{\rho}(y,z)|^{2}\,\operatorname{Re}\big(\mathcal{I}_{\rho}^{h}\partial^{k}\overline{\psi}_{\rho}-\overline{\psi}_{\rho}\partial^{k}\mathcal{I}_{\rho}^{h}\big)(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})\Big|\mathrm{d}t

(6.6)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\Big|\int(|\nabla_{y}|^{-2}\partial_{y}|\psi_{\rho}(y,z)|^{2}\cdot(\partial_{x}h_{\ll\rho}\cdot|\psi_{\rho}|^{2})(y,z^{\prime})\,\Big|\mathrm{d}t

(6.7)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\Big|\int|\nabla_{y}|^{-2}|\psi_{\rho}(y,z)|^{2}\cdot(\partial_{x}h_{\ll\rho}\cdot\partial_{x}|\psi_{\rho}|^{2})(y,z^{\prime})\,\Big|\mathrm{d}t

(6.8)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\Big|\int(|\nabla_{y}|^{-1}|\psi_{\rho}(y,z)|^{2}\cdot(\partial_{x}h_{\ll\rho}\cdot|\psi_{\rho}|^{2})(y,z^{\prime})\Big|\mathrm{d}t

(6.9)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\Big|\int R^{4}_{y}|\psi_{\rho}(y,z)|^{2}\cdot(h_{\ll\rho}\cdot|\psi_{\rho}|^{2})(y,z^{\prime}))\Big|,\mathrm{d}t

(6.10)

where
k
ranges from 1 to 3.
The term (
6.4
) is estimated as in Sections
4
and
5
, while (
6.5
) is treated as in (
4.4
) of Section
4
.

For (
6.6
), using (
2.41
) and integration by parts gives

(
6.6
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho\|\psi_{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\big(\|L(\psi_{\rho},P_{\rho}(\mathbf{U}\cdot\partial_{x}\psi+\psi u_{i}u_{j})+[Q_{\rho},B]\psi)\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}

(6.11)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\|L\big(\psi_{\rho},\partial_{\alpha}[P_{\rho},h^{\alpha\beta}]\partial_{\beta}\psi\big)\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\Big|\int(\partial a)(y-y^{\prime})\,|\psi_{\rho}(y,z)|^{2}

(6.12)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big[\partial_{x}h_{\ll\rho}\cdot\big(\psi_{\rho}\cdot[P_{\rho},B]\psi)+\partial_{\alpha}[P_{\rho},h^{\alpha\beta}]\partial_{\beta}\psi\big)\Big](y^{\prime},z^{\prime})\Big|\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\Big|\int(\partial^{2}a)(y-y^{\prime})\,|\psi_{\rho}(y,z)|^{2}

(6.13)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\cdot\big(\psi_{\rho}\cdot[P_{\rho},B]\psi+\psi_{\rho}\cdot\partial_{\alpha}[P_{\rho},h^{\alpha\beta}]\partial_{\beta}\psi\big)(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})\Big|\mathrm{d}t.

Term (
6.11
) is estimated similarly to (
6.4
) and (
6.5
); the same applies to (
6.12
) after taking the
L^{\infty}_{x}
norm of
\partial_{x}h_{\ll\rho}
.

Modulo zeroth-order Riesz transforms, which are bounded on the relevant Lorentz spaces, convolution with
\partial^{2}a
is represented by
|\nabla_{y}|^{-2}
. Factoring

|\nabla_{y}|^{-2}=|\nabla_{y}|^{-1/2}|\nabla_{y}|^{-3/2},

we estimate (
6.13
) as follows:

(
6.13
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\int\big\||\nabla_{y}|^{-1/2}|\psi_{\rho}(y,z)|^{2}\big\|_{L_{y}^{2,1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\cdot\big\||\nabla_{y}|^{-3/2}\Big(\psi_{\rho}\cdot[P_{\rho},B]\psi+\psi_{\rho}\cdot\partial_{\alpha}\big([P_{\rho},h^{\alpha\beta}]\partial_{\beta}\psi\big)\Big)(y,z^{\prime})\big\|_{L_{y}^{2,\infty}}\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\int\big\|\nabla_{y}|\psi_{\rho}(y,z)|^{2}\big\|_{L_{y}^{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\cdot\big\|\Big(\psi_{\rho}\cdot[P_{\rho},B]\psi+\psi_{\rho}\cdot\partial_{\alpha}\big([P_{\rho},h^{\alpha\beta}]\partial_{\beta}\psi\big)\Big)(\cdot,z^{\prime})\big\|_{L_{y}^{1}}\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho\|\psi_{\rho}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\big\|\psi_{\rho}\cdot[P_{\rho},B]\psi+\psi_{\rho}\cdot\partial_{\alpha}\big([P_{\rho},h^{\alpha\beta}]\partial_{\beta}\psi\big)\big\|_{L_{t,x}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{8}}\rho^{-4s+1-4\sigma}c_{\rho}^{4}(\sigma),

where
L^{p,q}
denotes the Lorentz space, and we have used the
Lorentz–Hölder, Lorentz–Sobolev, and endpoint
Hardy–Littlewood–Sobolev inequalities stated in
Subsection
2.1
.

The term (
6.10
) can be estimated by taking the
L^{\infty}_{x}
norm of
h
and is absorbed into (
6.3
) by the smallness condition.

It remains to estimate (
6.7
)–(
6.9
). We present the
details only for (
6.7
), since the other two terms are treated
similarly. By the Lorentz–Hölder, Lorentz–Sobolev, and endpoint
Hardy–Littlewood–Sobolev inequalities, we have

(
6.7
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\int\big\||\nabla_{y}|^{-1/2}\partial_{y}|\psi_{\rho}(y,z)|^{2}\big\|_{L_{y}^{2,1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\cdot\big\||\nabla_{y}|^{-3/2}\big(\partial_{x}h_{\ll\rho}\cdot|\psi_{\rho}|^{2}\big)(y,z^{\prime})\big\|_{L_{y}^{2,\infty}}\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\int\big\|\nabla_{y}\partial_{y}|\psi_{\rho}(y,z)|^{2}\big\|_{L_{y}^{1}}\big\|\big(\partial_{x}h_{\ll\rho}\cdot|\psi_{\rho}|^{2}\big)(\cdot,z^{\prime})\big\|_{L_{y}^{1}}\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho^{2}\|\psi_{\rho}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\big\|L(\partial_{x}h_{\ll\rho},\psi_{\rho},\psi_{\rho})\big\|_{L_{t,x}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho\|\psi_{\rho}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\big\|L(\partial_{x}h_{\ll\rho},\psi_{\rho},\partial_{x}\psi_{\rho})\big\|_{L_{t,x}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{8}}\rho^{-4s+1-4\sigma}c_{\rho}^{4}(\sigma),

where the last inequality then follows from Lemma
3.8
. This completes the proof.

7.
Elliptic estimates

In this section, we are going to prove (
1.33
) and (
1.32
)–(
1.35
) for
i\neq 1
or
j\neq 1
.
We first prove (
1.33
), namely

\displaystyle\left\|P_{\rho}u_{\mathfrak{T}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\lesssim\rho^{-s-1-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma),\mathfrak{T}=3,4,5.

Similar to (
3.30
), by the formula (
2.5
)–(
2.7
) for
L
notation, we can apply
P_{\rho}
to (
2.31
) to obtain

\displaystyle P_{\rho}u_{\mathfrak{T}}=

\displaystyle\,L(h_{\ll\rho},P_{\rho}u_{\mathfrak{T}})+\rho^{-1}L(P_{\rho}u_{3},P_{\ll\rho}u_{\mathfrak{T}})

\displaystyle\,+\rho^{-2}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}P_{\rho}L(P_{\nu}u_{3},\partial_{x}P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{\mathfrak{T}})+\rho^{-2}\partial_{x}P_{\rho}(u_{2}^{2})+\rho^{-2}P_{\rho}(u_{i}u_{j}u_{l}).

Taking
L^{\infty}_{t,x}
norm of
h_{\ll\rho}
,
P_{\ll\rho}u_{\mathfrak{T}}
, we estimate the first and second terms on the right-hand side of the above equation directly via (
3.2
) and (
1.28
).
By Remark
3.7
, the last term can be estimated in the same way as the second-last term, while the second-last term can be estimated using Lemma
3.4
. The estimate for the remaining third term is straightforward:

\displaystyle\rho^{-2}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\left\|P_{\rho}L(P_{\nu}u_{3},P_{\nu^{\prime}}\partial_{x}u_{\mathfrak{T}})\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\rho^{\frac{d}{2}-2}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\left\|L(P_{\nu}u_{3},P_{\nu^{\prime}}\partial_{x}u_{\mathfrak{T}})\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\rho^{\frac{d}{2}-2}\nu^{\prime}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\left\|P_{\nu}u_{3}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\left\|P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{\mathfrak{T}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\rho^{-s-1-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma).

Regarding (
1.32
)–(
1.35
) for
i\neq 1
or
j\neq 1,
the case
i=2\text{ or }j=2
can be proved similarly with other cases, thus we can further unify the elliptic equations (
2.30
) and (
2.31
) into a
more general equation

\displaystyle\Delta u_{\mathfrak{n}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{x}^{2}u_{1}+h\cdot\partial_{x}^{2}u_{\mathfrak{n}}+\partial_{x}h\cdot\partial_{x}u_{\mathfrak{n}}+\partial_{x}(u_{l}u_{k}),

(7.1)

where

\mathfrak{n}\in\{2,3,4,5\},\ l,k\in\{1,2,3,4,5\}.

The cubic terms
u_{k}u_{l}u_{m}
have been omitted, as they can always be estimated similarly to
u_{k}\cdot\partial_{x}u_{l}
using Remark
3.7
.

By the property of
L
notation and Bony’s decomposition, one can employ
\Delta^{-1}P_{\rho}
to (
7.1
) to get

\displaystyle\|P_{\rho}u_{\mathfrak{n}}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|L(h_{\ll\rho},P_{\sim\rho}u_{\mathfrak{n}})\|_{L^{2}}+\rho^{-1}\|L(P_{\sim\rho}u_{3},P_{\ll\rho}u_{\mathfrak{n}})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\|P_{\rho}L(P_{\nu}u_{3},P_{\nu^{\prime}}\partial_{x}u_{\mathfrak{n}})\|_{L^{2}}+\rho^{-1}\|P_{\rho}\left(u_{l}u_{k}\right)\|_{L^{2}}+\|P_{\rho}u_{1}\|_{L^{2}},

from which we immediately obtain (
1.32
) for
i=2,3,4,5
, noting that that the first term on the right-hand side of the above inequality can be estimated by taking
L^{\infty}
norm of
h_{\ll\rho}
and using (
3.2
). The term
\|P_{\rho}u_{1}\|_{L^{2}}
has already been estimated in Section
4
, while the remaining terms can be handled similarly to those in Lemma
3.10
.

Let us come to the bilinear estimate (
1.34
), i.e.

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau}\left\|\|P_{\mu}u_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{\gamma}}}\|P_{\rho,\gamma}u_{j}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{\gamma}}}

(7.2)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{-s}c_{\mu}(0)\rho^{-s-\frac{1}{2}-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma),\quad\text{ for }~~\mu\ll\rho,

for
i\neq 1
or
j\neq 1.
Without loss of generality, we may set
\gamma=1
.

We first consider the case
j\neq 1
. Denoting index
j
by
\mathfrak{n}
in this case, by the properties of the
L
notation and Bony’s decomposition, we apply
P_{\rho,1}
to (
7.1
) to obtain

\displaystyle\Delta P_{\rho,1}u_{\mathfrak{n}}=

\displaystyle\,\,\partial_{x}^{2}P_{\rho,1}u_{1}+P_{\rho,1}(P_{\ll\rho}h\cdot\partial_{x}^{2}P_{\sim\rho}u_{\mathfrak{n}}+P_{\sim\rho}h\cdot\partial_{x}^{2}P_{\ll\rho}u_{\mathfrak{n}})

\displaystyle\,+P_{\rho,1}(P_{\ll\rho}u_{3}\cdot\partial_{x}P_{\sim\rho}u_{\mathfrak{n}}+P_{\sim\rho}u_{3}\cdot\partial_{x}P_{\ll\rho}u_{\mathfrak{n}})

(7.3)

\displaystyle\,+\rho P_{\rho,1}(L(P_{\ll\rho}u_{l},P_{\sim\rho}u_{k})+L(P_{\sim\rho}u_{l},P_{\ll\rho}u_{k}))

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}P_{\rho,1}L(P_{\nu}u_{3},\partial_{x}P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{\mathfrak{n}})+\rho\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}P_{\rho,1}L(P_{\nu}u_{l},P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\partial_{x}^{2}P_{\rho,1}u_{1}+h_{\ll\rho}\cdot\partial_{x}^{2}P_{\rho,1}u_{l}+\rho L(P_{\ll\rho}u_{l},P_{\sim\rho}u_{k})

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}P_{\rho,1}L(P_{\nu}u_{3},\partial_{x}P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{\mathfrak{n}})+\rho\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}P_{\rho,1}L(P_{\nu}u_{l},P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{k}),

where
u_{3}=\partial_{x}h
and the Lemma
2.1
gives

\displaystyle[P_{\rho,1},P_{\ll\rho}h]\partial_{x}^{2}P_{\sim\rho}u_{l}=\rho L(P_{\ll\rho}u_{3},P_{\sim\rho}u_{l}).

Taking
u_{\mathfrak{n}}
as the second factor in
(
7.2
), substituting (
7.3
), and applying Hölder’s
and Bernstein’s inequalities together with
(
1.27
)–(
1.30
) and (
3.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\rho^{2}\sup_{\tau}\big\|\|P_{\mu}u_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\|P_{\rho,1}u_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{1}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\rho^{2}\sup_{\tau}\big\|\|P_{\mu}u_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\|P_{\rho,1}u_{1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{1}}}

\displaystyle\,+\|P_{\ll\rho}h\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\rho^{2}\sup_{\tau}\big\|\|P_{\mu}u_{l}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\|P_{\rho,1}u_{k}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{1}}}

\displaystyle+\rho\|P_{\mu}u_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}L^{\infty}_{x_{1}}}\cdot\|L(P_{\ll\rho}u_{l},P_{\sim\rho}u_{k})\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\|P_{\mu}u_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}L^{\infty}_{x_{1}}}\cdot\|P_{\rho,1}L(P_{\nu}u_{3},\partial_{x}P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{\mathfrak{n}})\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\|P_{\mu}u_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}L^{\infty}_{x_{1}}}\cdot\rho\|P_{\rho,1}L(P_{\nu}u_{l},P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{k})\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\rho^{2}\sup_{\tau}\big\|\|P_{\mu}u_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\|P_{\rho,1}u_{1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{1}}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{8}}\mu^{-s}c_{\mu}(0)\rho^{-s+\frac{3}{2}-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma).

(7.4)

Indeed, (
3.11
) gives

\displaystyle\rho\|P_{\mu}u_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}L^{\infty}_{x_{1}}}\cdot\|L(P_{\ll\rho}u_{l},P_{\sim\rho}u_{k})\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\rho\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|P_{\mu}u_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\cdot\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{4}}\sum_{\omega\ll\rho}\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho^{-s-\frac{1}{2}-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{8}}\mu^{-s}c_{\mu}(0)\rho^{-s+\frac{3}{2}-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma).

For the first high–high contribution, Hölder’s and Bernstein’s
inequalities, together with (
1.27
), (
1.28
), and
(
1.26
), yield

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\|P_{\mu}u_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}L^{\infty}_{x_{1}}}\cdot\|P_{\rho,1}L(P_{\nu}u_{3},\partial_{x}P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{\mathfrak{n}})\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

(7.5)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\|P_{\mu}u_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}L^{\infty}_{x_{1}}}\cdot\rho^{\frac{d}{2}}\|L(P_{\nu}u_{3},\partial_{x}P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{\mathfrak{n}})\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|P_{\mu}u_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\cdot\rho^{\frac{d}{2}}\|P_{\nu}u_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|\partial_{x}P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{\mathfrak{n}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}-s}c_{\mu}(0)\cdot\rho^{s+1}\nu^{-s-1-\sigma}c_{\nu}(\sigma)\cdot(\nu^{\prime})^{-s+1}c_{\nu^{\prime}}(0)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{8}}\mu^{-s}c_{\mu}(0)\rho^{-s+\frac{3}{2}-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma)

Similarly, for the second high–high contribution, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\|P_{\mu}u_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}L^{\infty}_{x_{1}}}\cdot\rho\|P_{\rho,1}L(P_{\nu}u_{l},P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{k})\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

(7.6)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\|P_{\mu}u_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\cdot\|P_{\rho,1}L(P_{\nu}u_{l},P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{k})\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,y}L^{1}_{z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\|P_{\mu}u_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\cdot\|P_{\nu}u_{l}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L_{z}^{2}}\|P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{k}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L_{z}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{-\frac{3}{8}}\rho^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\mu^{-s+\frac{1}{2}}c_{\mu}(0)\nu^{-s+\frac{1}{4}-\sigma}c_{\nu}(\sigma)(\nu^{\prime})^{-s+\frac{1}{4}}c_{\nu^{\prime}}(0)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{8}}\mu^{-s}c_{\mu}(0)\rho^{-s+\frac{3}{2}-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma).

Since we have already proved the bilinear estimate (
1.34
) for
i=j=1
, applying (
7.4
) yields (
7.2
) for
i=1
and
j\neq 1
. Consequently, it suffices to establish (
7.2
) for the case
i\neq 1
.

We now consider (
7.2
) when

i\neq 1
. Denoting
i=\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}
in this case,
by the property of
L
notation and Bony’s decomposition,
we can apply
P_{\mu}
to (
7.1
) to get

\displaystyle\Delta P_{\mu}u_{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle\,\partial_{x}^{2}P_{\mu}u_{1}+\mu^{2}L(h_{\ll\mu},P_{\sim\mu}u_{l})+\mu L(P_{\ll\mu}u_{l},P_{\sim\mu}u_{k})

(7.7)

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\rho\gg\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\mu}\nu^{\prime}P_{\mu}L(P_{\nu}u_{3},P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{\mathfrak{n}})+\sum_{\rho\gg\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\mu}\mu P_{\mu}L(P_{\nu}u_{l},P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{k})

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}P_{\mu}L(P_{\nu}u_{3},\partial_{x}P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{\mathfrak{n}})+\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\mu P_{\mu}L(P_{\nu}u_{l},P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{k})

for
l,k\in\{1,2,3,4,5\}
. Replacing
i
in (
7.2
) with
\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}
, we can plug the above equality into (
7.2
), then use
Hölder’s inequality, Bernstein’s inequality, (
3.2
), (
1.27
), (
1.29
), (
1.30
), (
7.2
) for
i=1
and similar analysis for (
7.5
)-(
7.6
) to obtain

\displaystyle\mu^{2}\sup_{\tau}\big\|\|P_{\mu}u_{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\|P_{\rho,1}u_{j}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{1}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\mu^{2}\sup_{\tau}\big\|\|P_{\mu}u_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\|P_{\rho,1}u_{j}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{1}}}

\displaystyle\,+\left(\mu^{2}\|h_{\ll\mu}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\mu\|P_{\leq\mu}u_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)\cdot\sup_{\tau}\big\|\|P_{\sim\mu}u_{l}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\|P_{\rho,1}u_{j}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{1}}}

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\rho\gg\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\mu}\mu^{\frac{d-1}{2}}(\nu^{\prime}+\mu)\sup_{\tau}\big\|\|L(P_{\nu}u_{l},P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{k})\|_{L^{1}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\|P_{\rho,1}u_{j}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{1}}}

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\nu^{\prime}\|P_{\mu}L(P_{\nu}u_{3},P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{\mathfrak{n}})\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\cdot\|P_{\rho,1}u_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x_{1}}L^{2}_{x_{1}}}

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\mu\|P_{\mu}L(P_{\nu}u_{l},P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{k})\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\cdot\|P_{\rho,1}u_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x_{1}}L^{2}_{x_{1}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{8}}\mu^{-s+2}c_{\mu}(0)\rho^{-s-\frac{1}{2}-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon^{-\frac{3}{8}}\mu^{2}c_{\mu}(0)\cdot\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}\mu^{-s}c_{\mu}(0)\rho^{-s-\frac{1}{2}-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\rho\gg\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\mu}\mu^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\nu^{\frac{3}{2}}\|P_{\nu}u_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\cdot\sup_{\tau}\big\|\|P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{k}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\|P_{\rho,1}u_{j}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{1}}}

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\nu^{\prime}\mu^{\frac{d}{2}}\|L(P_{\nu}u_{3},P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{\mathfrak{n}})\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{1}_{x}}\cdot\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\|P_{\rho}u_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\mu^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\|L(P_{\nu}u_{l},P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{k})\|_{L^{2}_{t,y}L^{1}_{z}}\cdot\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\|P_{\rho}u_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{8}}\mu^{-s+2}c_{\mu}(0)\rho^{-s-\frac{1}{2}-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon^{-\frac{3}{8}}\sum_{\rho\gg\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\mu}\mu^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\nu^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu^{-s}c_{\nu}(0)(\nu^{\prime})^{-s}c_{\nu^{\prime}}(0)\rho^{-s-\frac{1}{2}-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon^{-\frac{3}{8}}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\mu^{\frac{d}{2}}\nu^{-s-1}c_{\nu}(0)(\nu^{\prime})^{-s+1}c_{\nu^{\prime}}(0)\rho^{-s+\frac{1}{2}-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon^{-\frac{3}{8}}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\mu^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\nu^{-s+\frac{1}{4}}c_{\nu}(0)(\nu^{\prime})^{-s+\frac{1}{4}}c_{\nu^{\prime}}(0)\rho^{-s+\frac{1}{2}-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{8}}\mu^{-s+2}c_{\mu}(0)\rho^{-s-\frac{1}{2}-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma).

This completes the proof of (
7.2
) for
i\neq 1
or
j\neq 1
.

Finally, we prove the interaction Morawetz estimates (
1.35
) for
i=\mathfrak{n}\neq 1
, namely

\displaystyle\left\|P_{\rho}u_{\mathfrak{n}}\right\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}\lesssim\rho^{-s+\frac{1}{4}-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma).

Replacing
\mu
in (
7.7
) with
\rho
gives

\displaystyle\Delta P_{\rho}u_{\mathfrak{n}}=

\displaystyle\,\partial_{x}^{2}P_{\rho}u_{1}+\rho^{2}L(P_{\leq\rho}h,P_{\sim\rho}u_{l})+\rho L(P_{\ll\rho}u_{l},P_{\sim\rho}u_{k})

(7.8)

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}P_{\rho}L(P_{\nu}u_{3},\partial_{x}P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{\mathfrak{n}})+\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\rho P_{\rho}L(P_{\nu}u_{l},P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{k}),

which together with (
1.30
) for
i=1
, Bernstein inequality and Hölder’s inequality yields

\displaystyle\rho^{2}\left\|P_{\rho}u_{\mathfrak{n}}\right\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\rho^{2}\left\|P_{\rho}u_{1}\right\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}+\rho^{2}\|h_{\ll\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\left\|P_{\sim\rho}u_{l}\right\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}

\displaystyle\,+\rho\|P_{\ll\rho}u_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\left\|P_{\sim\rho}u_{k}\right\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}(\nu^{\prime}+\rho)\rho^{\frac{d-3}{2}+\frac{3}{2}}\left\|L(P_{\nu}u_{l},P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{k})\right\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\frac{4}{3}}_{y}L^{1}_{z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{4}}\rho^{-s+\frac{9}{4}-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma)+\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\nu^{\prime}\rho^{\frac{d}{2}}\|P_{\nu}u_{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\left\|P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{k}\right\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{4}}\rho^{-s+\frac{9}{4}-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma)+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\rho^{\frac{d}{2}}\nu^{-s}c_{\nu}(0)(\nu^{\prime})^{-s+\frac{5}{4}-\sigma}c_{\nu^{\prime}}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{4}}\rho^{-s+\frac{9}{4}-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma).

This is exactly (
1.35
) for
i=\mathfrak{n}\neq 1
.

8.
Estimates for
B

It remains to estimate the unknown function
B
. As mentioned in Section
1
, the scale of
B
is different from
u_{i}\,(i=1,2,3,4,5)
and it requires a more refined analysis based on (
2.32
).

Applying
P_{\rho}
to
(
2.32
) and taking
L^{2}_{t,x}
norm, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}B_{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|P_{\rho}\partial_{x}(u_{i}u_{j})\|_{L_{t,x}^{2}}

(8.1)

\displaystyle\,+\rho^{-1}\|P_{\sim\rho}(\partial_{x}u_{5}\cdot\partial_{x}u_{4})\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

(8.2)

\displaystyle\,+\|P_{\rho}(u_{3}\cdot B)+\rho^{-1}P_{\sim\rho}(u_{3}\cdot\partial_{x}B)\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

(8.3)

\displaystyle\,+\|P_{\rho}(u_{i}u_{j}u_{l})+\rho^{-1}P_{\sim\rho}(u_{i}u_{j}\partial_{x}u_{l}+u_{i}u_{j}u_{k}u_{l})\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}.

(8.4)

The high order terms in (
8.4
) can be estimated in a same manner as the quadratic terms, as the analysis in Remark
3.7
. The estimate for (
8.1
) follows directly from Lemma
3.4
.
For (
8.2
)–(
8.4
), it suffices to estimate only the terms where the derivative is applied to the product before frequency localization, i.e., terms of the form
\rho^{-1}P_{\sim\rho}(\partial_{x}(\cdot)\partial_{x}(\cdot))
and
\rho^{-1}P_{\sim\rho}(\cdots\partial_{x}(\cdot)\cdots)
. Indeed, in the low–high and high–low frequency interaction regimes, the estimates for
P_{\rho}(\partial_{x}(uv))
and
P_{\rho}(u\partial_{x}v)
are equivalent up to constants. In the high–high regime, however, the latter case—where the derivative falls on the product—is more singular and therefore dominates the analysis; controlling it suffices to obtain the desired bounds for the former.

For (
8.2
), we estimate

\displaystyle\|\rho^{-1}P_{\sim\rho}(\partial_{x}u_{5}\partial_{x}u_{4})\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\rho\left\|L(P_{\ll\rho}u_{4},P_{\rho}u_{5})\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}+\sum_{\nu^{\prime}\sim\nu\gtrsim\rho}\nu^{\prime}\nu\rho^{\frac{d}{2}-1}\left\|P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{4}P_{\nu}u_{5}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\left\|P_{\mu}u_{4}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\rho\left\|P_{\rho}u_{5}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}+\sum_{\nu^{\prime}\sim\nu\gtrsim\rho}\nu^{\prime}\nu\rho^{\frac{d}{2}-1}\left\|P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{4}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\left\|P_{\nu}u_{5}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\mu^{\frac{d}{2}-s}c_{\mu}(0)\rho^{-s-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma)+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sum_{\nu^{\prime}\sim\nu\gtrsim\rho}\rho^{\frac{d}{2}-1}(\nu^{\prime})^{-s+1}c_{\nu^{\prime}}(0)\nu^{-s-\sigma}c_{\nu}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{4}}\rho^{-s+1-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma).

For (
8.3
), we estimate

\displaystyle\|\rho^{-1}P_{\sim\rho}(u_{3}\partial_{x}B)\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|L(P_{\ll\rho}u_{3},B_{\rho})\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}+\|L(P_{\ll\rho}B,P_{\rho}u_{3})\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}+\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\nu\rho^{\frac{d}{2}-1}\|P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{3}\cdot B_{\nu}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\left\|P_{\mu}u_{3}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\left\|B_{\rho}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}+\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\left\|B_{\mu}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\left\|P_{\rho}u_{3}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{\nu^{\prime}\sim\nu\gtrsim\rho}\nu\rho^{\frac{d}{2}-1}\left\|P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{3}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\left\|B_{\nu}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\mu^{\frac{d}{2}-s}c_{\mu}(0)\rho^{-s-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma)+\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\mu^{\frac{d}{2}-s}c_{\mu}(0)\rho^{-s-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sum_{\nu^{\prime}\sim\nu\gtrsim\rho}\rho^{\frac{d}{2}-1}(\nu^{\prime})^{-s}c_{\nu^{\prime}}(0)\nu^{-s+1-\sigma}c_{\nu}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{4}}\rho^{-s+1-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma).

Collecting estimates of (
8.1
)–(
8.4
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|B_{\rho}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\lesssim\rho^{-s-\sigma}c_{\rho}(\sigma),

which is exactly (
1.36
).

Finally, combining the estimates obtained in Section
4
-
8
, we have finished the proof of Theorem
1.3
.

9.
Global Well-Posedness in the Critical Besov Space

In this section, we complete the proof of global well-posedness for the Schrödinger–elliptic system
(
1.16
)–(
1.18
). Together with the initial gauge construction and the reconstruction of the geometric flow, this yields global well-posedness for (
1.2
) in the critical Besov space under the harmonic/Coulomb gauge formulation.
The argument relies on the uniform a priori estimates at the critical regularity obtained in Theorem
1.3
and a weak difference estimate one derivative below the critical level obtained in this section. To construct solutions for rough initial data, we apply these estimates to a sequence of smooth approximations. The critical estimates provide uniform control and propagate the frequency envelopes, while the weak difference estimate shows that the approximate solutions form a Cauchy sequence in a weaker topology. The same difference estimate also yields uniqueness and continuous dependence on the initial data.

We assume throughout in this section that

s=\frac{d}{2}-1,~d\geq 5,

and

\|\psi_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}\leq\varepsilon,

where
\varepsilon>0
is sufficiently small. Let

\left(\psi^{(a)},\lambda^{(a)},h^{(a)},V^{(a)},A^{(a)},B^{(a)}\right),\quad a=1,2,

be two smooth compatible solutions of the Schrödinger–elliptic system (
1.16
)–(
1.18
) on a time interval
I=[0,T].

We further set

\left(u_{1}^{(a)},u_{2}^{(a)},u_{3}^{(a)},u_{4}^{(a)},u_{5}^{(a)}\right):=\left(\psi^{(a)},\lambda^{(a)},\partial_{x}h^{(a)},V^{(a)},A^{(a)}\right)

and define

\delta u_{i}:=u_{i}^{(1)}-u_{i}^{(2)},~~\delta B:=B^{(1)}-B^{(2)},

for
i=1,2,3,4,5
.
In particular, we write

w:=u_{1}^{(1)}-u_{1}^{(2)}=\delta\psi:=\psi^{(1)}-\psi^{(2)}.

Assume that both solutions satisfy the critical a priori bounds with a common
slowly varying frequency envelope
\{c_{\rho}\}
satisfying properties as (
1.24
)–(
1.26
) and a priori estimates as

\displaystyle\|P_{\rho}u_{i}^{(a)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(I)}\lesssim\rho^{-s}c_{\rho},

(Ea1)

\displaystyle\|P_{\rho}u_{\mathfrak{T}}^{(a)}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}(I)}\lesssim\rho^{-s-1}c_{\rho},\qquad\mathfrak{T}\in\{3,4,5\},

(Ea2)

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau}\left\|\|P_{\mu}u_{i}^{(a)}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{\gamma}}}\|P_{\rho,\gamma}u_{j}^{(a),\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{\gamma}}(I)}\lesssim\mu^{-s}c_{\mu}\,\rho^{-s-\frac{1}{2}}c_{\rho},

(Ea3)

\displaystyle\|P_{\rho}u_{i}^{(a)}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}(I)}\lesssim\rho^{-s+\frac{1}{4}}c_{\rho},

(Ea4)

\displaystyle\|P_{\rho}B^{(a)}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}(I)}\lesssim\rho^{-s}c_{\rho},

(Ea5)

where
\mu\ll\rho;i,j\in\{1,2,3,4,5\};\mathfrak{T}\in\{3,4,5\}
.

We measure the difference between the two solutions in a topology one
derivative below the critical regularity, namely in

\dot{B}^{s-1}_{2,1}
. For each dyadic frequency
\rho
, let

D_{\rho}(I)
denote the aggregate of the normalized frequency localized
quantities at frequency
\rho
arising from the energy, bilinear,
interaction Morawetz, elliptic, and
B
-estimates. We then define
the weak difference control norm by

D(I):=\sum_{\rho}D_{\rho}(I).

Associated with these dyadic quantities, we define the frequency envelope
by

d_{\rho}:=\frac{1}{D(I)}\sum_{\nu}2^{-\delta\left|\log_{2}(\rho/\nu)\right|}D_{\nu}(I),

where
0<\delta\ll 1
is fixed sufficiently small. When
D(I)=0
, we
adopt the convention that
d_{\rho}=0
for every
\rho
. By construction,

D_{\rho}(I)\leq D(I)d_{\rho}.

Moreover,

\sum_{\rho}d_{\rho}\lesssim_{\delta}1,

and the envelope satisfies the slow-variation property

\displaystyle d_{\mu}\lesssim\left(\frac{\max\{\mu,\rho\}}{\min\{\mu,\rho\}}\right)^{\delta}d_{\rho},\qquad\text{for all dyadic }\mu,\rho.

(9.1)

After a harmless normalization, we may therefore assume that

\sum_{\rho}d_{\rho}\leq 1.

In particular, if
\mu\ll\rho
, then

d_{\mu}\lesssim\left(\frac{\rho}{\mu}\right)^{\delta}d_{\rho}.

With this choice of frequency envelope, the frequency localized bounds used
in the bootstrap argument take the following form:

\displaystyle\|w_{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(I)}\leq D\,\rho^{-s+1}d_{\rho},

(UA1)

\displaystyle\|w_{\rho}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}(I)}\leq D\,\rho^{-s+\frac{5}{4}}d_{\rho},

(UA2)

\displaystyle\|P_{\rho}\delta u_{\mathfrak{n}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(I)}\leq D\,\rho^{-s+1}d_{\rho},\qquad

(UA3)

\displaystyle\|P_{\rho}\delta u_{\mathfrak{T}}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}(I)}\leq D\,\rho^{-s}d_{\rho},\qquad

(UA4)

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau}\left\|\|P_{\mu}\delta u_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{\gamma}}}\|P_{\rho,\gamma}u_{j}^{(a),\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{\gamma}}(I)}\leq D\,\mu^{-s+1}d_{\mu}\,\rho^{-s-\frac{1}{2}}c_{\rho},

(UA5)

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau}\left\|\|P_{\mu}u_{i}^{(a)}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{\gamma}}}\|P_{\rho,\gamma}\delta u_{j}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{\gamma}}(I)}\leq D\,\mu^{-s}c_{\mu}\,\rho^{-s+\frac{1}{2}}d_{\rho},

(UA6)

\displaystyle\|P_{\rho}\delta u_{\mathfrak{n}}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}(I)}\leq D\,\rho^{-s+\frac{5}{4}}d_{\rho},

(UA7)

\displaystyle\|P_{\rho}\delta B\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\leq D\,\rho^{-s+1}d_{\rho},

(UA8)

where
\mu\ll\rho;i,j\in\{1,2,3,4,5\};\mathfrak{T}\in\{3,4,5\};\mathfrak{n}=2,3,4,5;a=1,2
and
w_{\rho}=P_{\rho}w
.

We write
D=D(I)
for the weak difference control norm associated with
(
UA1
)–(
UA8
). More precisely,
D(I)
is the sum of the
normalized dyadic quantities arising from the energy, bilinear, interaction
Morawetz, elliptic, and
B
-field estimates. For example, the components
corresponding to (
UA1
) and (
UA3
) are, respectively,

\displaystyle D_{E}:=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\rho}\rho^{2s-2}\|w_{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(I)}^{2},

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{ell}}(I):=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\mathfrak{n}=2}^{5}\sum_{\rho}\rho^{2s-2}\|P_{\rho}\delta u_{\mathfrak{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}(I)}^{2}.

The remaining components are defined analogously by applying the
normalizations appearing in (
UA2
) and
(
UA4
)–(
UA8
). Consequently, each normalized dyadic quantity is
controlled pointwise by
D(I)d_{\rho}
, with the appropriate background
frequency envelope
c_{\rho}
in the mixed bilinear estimates
(
UA5
) and (
UA6
).

We measure the initial separation of the two solutions by

D(0):=\|w(0)\|_{\dot{B}^{s-1}_{2,1}}.
The corresponding elliptic differences at
t=0
are also controlled by
D(0)
, since the elliptic constraint map is locally Lipschitz in the weak topology.

The uniqueness argument is therefore reduced to a stability estimate for the
full difference norm on
I
. More precisely, we prove that the
difference remains controlled by its initial size, up to an error carrying a
positive power of the small critical norm of the two background solutions.

Proposition 9.1

(Weak Lipschitz dependence)
.

There exist constants
C>0
and
\kappa>0
, independent of
T
, such
that

D(I)\leq CD(0)+C\varepsilon^{\kappa}D(I).

Consequently, if
\varepsilon>0
is sufficiently small, then

D(I)\lesssim D(0).

The proof follows the frequency localized framework developed for
Theorem
1.3
, but is carried out one derivative below the critical
regularity and applied to the difference system. In this setting, the bounds
(
Ea1
)–(
UA8
) for the background solutions and their differences
play the role of the a priori assumptions (
1.27
)–(
1.31
).
After transferring the relevant frequency envelopes to the reference
frequency through (
9.1
), the dyadic summations are handled by
the sign criterion introduced at the beginning of
Section
3
.

Most nonlinear contributions can therefore be estimated by suitable
difference versions of the arguments already used for
Theorem
1.3
. We shall concentrate on the terms that contain a
genuine difference interaction, an additional derivative loss, or a
coefficient difference and hence require a modification of the previous
estimates.

We first derive several consequences of the bootstrap assumptions for the
background and difference variables. These bounds will be used repeatedly in
the energy, bilinear, interaction Morawetz, and elliptic estimates associated
with (
Ea1
)–(
UA8
).

9.1.
Preliminary estimates from assumptions

We first record the dyadic
L^{\infty}_{t,x}
bounds. The background
variables
u_{i}
carry one frequency power, while the background field

B
carries two. Since the differences are measured one derivative below
the critical regularity,
\delta u_{i}
and
\delta B
carry two and three
frequency powers, respectively.

Lemma 9.2
.

Assume (
Ea1
)–(
UA8
) hold. Then, we have

\displaystyle\|P_{\mu}u_{i}^{(a)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}}\lesssim\mu c_{\mu},\qquad\|P_{\mu}B^{(a)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}}\lesssim\mu c_{\mu}^{2},

(9.2)

\displaystyle\|P_{\mu}\delta u_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}}\lesssim D\mu^{2}d_{\mu},\qquad\|P_{\mu}\delta B\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}}\lesssim D\mu^{3}d_{\mu},

(9.3)

for
a\in\{1,2\};i\in\{1,2,3,4,5\}.

The next lemma converts the space–time bootstrap bounds into the mixed norms
that will be used to control low-frequency factors in the difference
equations.

Lemma 9.3
.

Assume (
Ea1
)–(
UA8
) hold. Then, we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}P_{\ll\rho}u_{i}^{(a)}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{\infty}_{z}}\lesssim\varepsilon\rho^{\frac{3}{4}}

(9.4)

\displaystyle\|w_{\rho}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{\infty}_{z}}\lesssim\rho^{\frac{3}{4}}Dd_{\rho},

(9.5)

\displaystyle\|\delta B_{\ll\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{t,y}L^{\infty}_{z}}\lesssim\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}Dd_{\rho}

(9.6)

We finally record several anisotropic bilinear estimates. They are the
difference analogues of the corresponding bounds used in the a priori
argument, but one factor is now measured by the difference envelope

d_{\rho}
.

Lemma 9.4
.

Assume (
Ea1
)–(
UA8
) hold. Then, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma=1}^{d}\big\|\|P_{\ll\rho}\psi^{(a)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\widehat{x}_{\gamma}}}\|\overline{w}_{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{\gamma}}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{\gamma}}}\lesssim\varepsilon D\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho^{-s+\frac{1}{2}}d_{\rho},

(9.7)

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sup_{\tau}\left\|\|L(\delta h_{\ll\mu},P_{\mu}u_{\mathfrak{T}}^{(a)})\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\psi_{\sim\rho,1}^{(b)}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|w_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\right\|_{L^{1}_{t,y}}

(9.8)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,D^{2}\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\omega\ll\mu}\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho^{-2s}c_{\mu}c_{\rho}d_{\omega}d_{\rho},

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sup_{\tau}\left\|\|L(P_{\mu}\delta h,P_{\ll\mu}u_{\mathfrak{T}}^{(a)})\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\psi_{\sim\rho,1}^{(b)}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|w_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\right\|_{L^{1}_{t,y}}

(9.9)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,D^{2}\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\omega\ll\mu}\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho^{-2s}c_{\mu}c_{\rho}d_{\omega}d_{\rho},

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sup_{\tau}\left\|\|L(P_{\ll\mu}\delta u_{i},P_{\mu}u_{j}^{(a)})\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\psi_{\sim\rho,1}^{(b)}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|w_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\right\|_{L^{1}_{t,y}}

(9.10)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{2}D^{2}\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\omega\ll\mu}\omega^{\frac{3}{2}}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}d_{\omega}\rho^{-2s}d_{\rho},

where
a\neq b,a,b=1,2;i,j\in\{1,2,3,4,5\}
and we set
y_{1}=x_{1}
in
y=(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3})
.

The first two lemmas follow directly from the bootstrap assumptions and
Bernstein’s inequality. The proof of Lemma
9.4
illustrates the
anisotropic bilinear argument that will be used repeatedly below, so we
include the details.

Proof.

(
9.8
) can be estimated just as (3.10). Indeed

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sup_{\tau}\left\|\|L(\delta h_{\ll\mu},P_{\mu}u_{\mathfrak{T}}^{(2)})\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\psi_{\sim\rho,1}^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|w_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\right\|_{L^{1}_{t,y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\omega\ll\mu}\omega^{-1}\sup_{\tau}\left\|\|L(P_{\omega}\delta u_{3},P_{\mu}u_{\mathfrak{T}}^{(2)})\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\psi_{\sim\rho,1}^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|w_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\right\|_{L^{1}_{t,y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\omega\ll\mu}\omega^{\frac{d-3}{2}}\mu^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\big\|\|P_{\omega}\delta u_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\|\psi_{\sim\rho,1}^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{1}}}\big\|\|P_{\mu}u_{\mathfrak{T}}^{(2)}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\|w_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{1}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\omega\ll\mu}\omega^{s-\frac{1}{2}}\mu^{s+\frac{1}{2}}D\omega^{-s+1}d_{\omega}\rho^{-s-\frac{1}{2}}c_{\rho}D\mu^{-s}c_{\mu}\rho^{-s+\frac{1}{2}}d_{\rho},

which gives (
9.8
).

The estimate (
9.9
) is obtained in the same way, but with
the frequency-
\mu
difference factor placed in the first bilinear norm
and the lower-frequency background factor placed in the second one. More
precisely,

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sup_{\tau}\left\|\|L(P_{\mu}\delta h,P_{\ll\mu}u_{\mathfrak{T}}^{(2)})\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\psi_{\sim\rho,1}^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|w_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\right\|_{L^{1}_{t,y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\omega\ll\mu}\rho\mu^{\frac{d-3}{2}}\omega^{\frac{d-1}{2}}D\mu^{-s+1}d_{\mu}\rho^{-s-\frac{1}{2}}c_{\rho}D\omega^{-s}c_{\omega}\rho^{-s+\frac{1}{2}}d_{\rho}

The remaining estimates follow from the same anisotropic decomposition.
For (
9.7
), one places the low-frequency background factor
in
L^{\infty}_{\widehat{x}_{\gamma}}
and pairs it with
w_{\rho}
through
the difference bilinear bootstrap bound. For
(
9.10
), one instead places the low-frequency difference
factor in the corresponding anisotropic norm and pairs the
frequency-
\mu
background factor with
w_{\rho}
. The additional
smallness follows from the background frequency envelopes. This completes
the proof.

∎

9.2.
Difference energy estimate

As (
1.16
), the equation for
\psi^{(a)}
, can be written in divergence form as

\sqrt{-1}\partial_{t}\psi^{(a)}+\partial_{\alpha}\big(g^{\alpha\beta,(a)}\partial_{\beta}\psi^{(a)}\big)=\mathcal{N}^{(a)},

where
a=1,2
.
Subtracting the two equations and using
g^{\alpha\beta,(a)}=\delta^{\alpha\beta}+h^{\alpha\beta,(a)}
, we obtain

\sqrt{-1}\partial_{t}w+\partial_{\alpha}\big(g^{\alpha\beta,(1)}\partial_{\beta}w\big)=\delta\mathcal{N}-\partial_{\alpha}\big(\delta h^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\psi^{(2)}\big),

where

\delta\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}^{(1)}-\mathcal{N}^{(2)}.

Applying
P_{\rho}
and commuting
P_{\rho}
past the low-frequency coefficient yield

\displaystyle\sqrt{-1}\partial_{t}w_{\rho}+\partial_{\alpha}\big(g^{\alpha\beta,(1)}\partial_{\beta}w_{\rho}\big)=

\displaystyle\,P_{\rho}\delta\mathcal{N}-P_{\rho}\partial_{\alpha}\big(\delta h^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\psi^{(2)}\big)

(9.11)

\displaystyle\,-\partial_{\alpha}\big([P_{\rho},h^{\alpha\beta,(1)}]\partial_{\beta}w\big):=\mathfrak{N}_{\rho}.

As in Section
4
, we multiply (
9.11
) by

\overline{w}_{\rho}
, take the imaginary part, and integrate over

[0,t]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Multiplying the resulting identity by

\rho^{2s-2}
and summing over
\rho
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\rho}\rho^{2s-2}\|w_{\rho}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\leq\sum_{\rho}\rho^{2s-2}\|w_{\rho}(0)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+2\sum_{\rho}\rho^{2s-2}\mathcal{E}_{\rho}(t),

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\rho}(t):=

\displaystyle\,\Big|\operatorname{Im}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}P_{\rho}\delta\mathcal{N}\cdot\overline{w}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\Big|

(9.12)

\displaystyle\,+\Big|\operatorname{Im}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}P_{\rho}\partial_{\alpha}\big(\delta h^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\psi^{(2)}\big)\cdot\overline{w}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\Big|

(9.13)

\displaystyle\,+\Big|\operatorname{Im}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{\alpha}\big([P_{\rho},h^{\alpha\beta,(1)}]\partial_{\beta}w\big)\cdot\overline{w}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\Big|.

(9.14)

The initial term is bounded by
D(0)^{2}
. Therefore it remains to prove

\displaystyle\sum_{\rho}\rho^{2s-2}\mathcal{E}_{\rho}(t)\lesssim\varepsilon D^{2}.

(9.15)

It is enough to prove that each contribution to
\mathcal{E}_{\rho}
is either
of the pointwise summable form

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\rho}(t)\lesssim\varepsilon D^{2}\rho^{-2s+2}d_{\rho}^{2},

(9.16)

or of the following non-pointwise but summable envelope interaction form:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathcal{E}_{\rho}(t)\lesssim D^{2}\rho^{-2s+2}c_{\rho}d_{\rho}\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}c_{\mu}d_{\mu},\vskip 11.38092pt\\
\mathcal{E}_{\rho}(t)\lesssim D^{2}\rho^{-2s+2}c_{\rho}d_{\rho}\sum_{\nu\gtrsim\rho}c_{\nu}d_{\nu},\end{cases}

(9.17)

Both types of terms are summable after multiplication by

\rho^{2s-2}
. Indeed,
the terms as (
9.17
) are harmless only after summing in
\rho
, since

\displaystyle\max\left\{\sum_{\rho}c_{\rho}d_{\rho}\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}c_{\mu}d_{\mu},\quad\sum_{\rho}c_{\rho}d_{\rho}\sum_{\nu\gtrsim\rho}c_{\nu}d_{\nu}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\sum_{\rho}c_{\rho}d_{\rho}\Big)^{2}\lesssim\Big(\sum_{\rho}c_{\rho}\Big)^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{2}.

Then (
9.15
) follows.

We first treat (
9.14
) and (
9.12
). Most of their
contributions can be controlled by suitable adaptations of the corresponding
arguments in the proof of Theorem
1.3
.

For (
9.14
), the integration by parts and Bony’s decomposition of commutator (
2.11
) show

\displaystyle\eqref{UA1.term3}=

\displaystyle\,\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}[P_{\rho},h^{\alpha\beta,(1)}]\partial_{\beta}w\,\partial_{\alpha}\overline{w}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\,d\tau\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}L(\partial_{x}P_{\ll\rho}h^{\alpha\beta,(1)},w_{\sim\rho})\cdot\partial_{\alpha}\overline{w}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Big|

(9.18)

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}L(\partial_{\beta}P_{\ll\rho}w,P_{\sim\rho}h^{\alpha\beta,(1)})\cdot\partial_{\alpha}\overline{w}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Big|

(9.19)

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\sum_{\nu^{\prime}\sim\nu\gtrsim\rho}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}P_{\rho}L(P_{\nu}h^{\alpha\beta,(1)},\partial_{\beta}w_{\nu^{\prime}})\cdot\partial_{\alpha}\overline{w}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Big|.

(9.20)

The (
9.18
)–(
9.20
) can be bounded just as (
4.4
) -(
4.6
) by replacing
\psi
in (
4.4
)–(
4.6
) to
w
in (
9.18
)–(
9.20
) and using the assumption (
Ea1
)–(
UA8
). For instance, the same
argument used for (
3.39
), together with
(
9.5
) and (
9.1
), yields

\displaystyle\eqref{UA1.term3.t3}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\nu^{\prime}\sim\nu\gtrsim\rho}\rho\big\|\partial_{x}h^{(1)}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\left\|w_{\nu^{\prime}}\right\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}\|w_{\rho}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{\infty}_{z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon D^{2}\rho^{-2s+2}d_{\rho}^{2}.

Here, the
scale invariant total frequency power of the expression is
-2s+2
, by (
9.5
), the
factor

\|w_{\rho}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{\infty}_{z}}\lesssim\rho^{\frac{3}{4}}Dd_{\rho}

contributes
\rho^{3/4}
, and excluding the extra
\rho
, the residual high-frequency power for high frequency
\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}
is

-2s+\frac{1}{4}
, which is negative since
s=\frac{d}{2}-1\geq 1
.
Thus,
d_{\mu}
can be transformed into
d_{\rho}
by (
9.1
) and the frequency can be converted to the
\rho
, as
\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho
. This is precisely an application of the dyadic summation principle stated after Proposition
9.1
.

We next turn to (
9.12
). We first verify that the Gauss structure
is preserved after taking differences, in the same way as in the proof of
Lemma
3.5
. Set

\displaystyle\delta(\lambda\wedge\lambda):=(\lambda^{(1)}\wedge\lambda^{(1)})-(\lambda^{(2)}\wedge\lambda^{(2)}).

(9.21)

By definition of (
1.11
), we have

\displaystyle\delta(\lambda\wedge\lambda)_{\gamma\sigma}=

\displaystyle\,\delta\lambda_{\gamma\sigma}\overline{\psi^{(1)}}+\lambda_{\gamma\sigma}^{(2)}\overline{w}-\delta\lambda_{\alpha\gamma}\overline{(\lambda^{(1)})^{\alpha}_{\sigma}}-\lambda_{\alpha\gamma}^{(2)}\overline{\delta\bigl(\lambda^{\alpha}_{\sigma}\bigr)},

(9.22)

where

\displaystyle\delta\bigl(\lambda^{\alpha}_{\sigma}\bigr)=

\displaystyle\,g^{\alpha\beta,1}\delta\lambda_{\beta\sigma}+\delta g^{\alpha\beta}\lambda_{\beta\sigma}^{(2)}.

(9.23)

Consequently, modulo the cubic term

\lambda^{(2)}\cdot\delta g\cdot\lambda^{(2)},

the quadratic part of (
9.22
) is the polarized
Gauss interaction

\displaystyle\delta\lambda_{\gamma\sigma}\overline{\psi^{(1)}}+\lambda_{\gamma\sigma}^{(2)}\overline{w}-\delta\lambda_{\alpha\gamma}\overline{(\lambda^{(1)})^{\alpha}_{\sigma}}-\lambda_{\alpha\gamma}^{(2)}g_{1}^{\alpha\beta}\overline{\delta\lambda_{\beta\sigma}}.

Subtracting the Codazzi equations (
1.7
) for the two solutions gives
schematically

\displaystyle\partial_{\beta}\delta\lambda_{\gamma\sigma}=

\displaystyle\,\partial_{\sigma}\delta\lambda_{\gamma\beta}+L\bigl((u_{3}^{(1)},A^{(1)}),\delta\lambda\bigr)

(9.24)

\displaystyle+L\bigl((\delta u_{3},\delta A),\lambda^{(2)}\bigr)+L\bigl(\delta h,\partial_{x}\lambda^{(2)}\bigr)+u_{i}^{(a)}u_{j}^{(b)}\delta u_{l},

while the contracted difference Codazzi equation takes the form

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}\delta\bigl(\lambda^{\alpha}_{\sigma}\bigr)-\partial_{\sigma}w=

\displaystyle\,L\bigl((u_{3}^{(1)},A^{(1)}),\delta\lambda\bigr)

(9.25)

\displaystyle+L\bigl((\delta u_{3},\delta A),\lambda^{(2)}\bigr)+L\bigl(\delta h,\partial_{x}\lambda^{(2)}\bigr)+u_{i}^{(a)}u_{j}^{(b)}\delta u_{l},

where
a,b\in\{1,2\}
.

For
\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho
, we decompose the difference Gauss
interaction as

\displaystyle P_{\mu}\delta\bigl(\lambda_{\nu}\wedge\lambda_{\nu^{\prime}}\bigr)=

\displaystyle\,P_{\mu}\left[(P_{\nu}\delta\lambda_{\gamma\sigma})(P_{\nu^{\prime}}\overline{\psi^{(1)}})-(P_{\nu}\delta\lambda_{\alpha\gamma})P_{\nu^{\prime}}\overline{(\lambda^{(1)})^{\alpha}_{\sigma}}\right]

\displaystyle+P_{\mu}\left[(P_{\nu}\lambda_{\gamma\sigma}^{(2)})(P_{\nu^{\prime}}\overline{w})-(P_{\nu}\lambda_{\alpha\gamma}^{(2)})P_{\nu^{\prime}}\overline{\delta(\lambda^{\alpha}_{\sigma})}\right]

\displaystyle+P_{\mu}L\bigl(\delta h,\lambda^{(2)},\lambda^{(2)}\bigr).

(9.26)

The last term arises from

\delta(\lambda^{\alpha}_{\sigma})=g_{1}^{\alpha\beta}\delta\lambda_{\beta\sigma}+\delta g^{\alpha\beta}\lambda_{\beta\sigma}^{(2)}.

Thus, the first two lines of (
9.26
) are the
quadratic polarized Gauss interactions, whereas the contribution involving

\delta g
is cubic.

Using (
3.21
)
and the difference Codazzi identities

\displaystyle\partial_{\beta}\delta\lambda_{\gamma\sigma}=

\displaystyle\,\partial_{\sigma}\delta\lambda_{\gamma\beta}+L\bigl((u_{3}^{(1)},A^{(1)}),\delta\lambda\bigr)

\displaystyle+L\bigl((\delta u_{3},\delta A),\lambda^{(2)}\bigr)+L\bigl(\delta h,\partial_{x}\lambda^{(2)}\bigr),

(9.27)
