\displaystyle\partial_{\beta}\delta\lambda_{\alpha\gamma}=

\displaystyle\,\partial_{\alpha}\delta\lambda_{\beta\gamma}+L\bigl((u_{3}^{(1)},A^{(1)}),\delta\lambda\bigr)

\displaystyle+L\bigl((\delta u_{3},\delta A),\lambda^{(2)}\bigr)+L\bigl(\delta h,\partial_{x}\lambda^{(2)}\bigr),

(9.28)

we obtain

\displaystyle P_{\mu}\left[(P_{\nu}\delta\lambda_{\gamma\sigma})(P_{\nu^{\prime}}\overline{\psi^{(1)}})-(P_{\nu}\delta\lambda_{\alpha\gamma})P_{\nu^{\prime}}\overline{(\lambda^{(1)})^{\alpha}_{\sigma}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\nu^{-1}P_{\mu}\left[\partial_{\sigma}\bigl(\widetilde{P}_{\nu,\beta}\delta\lambda_{\gamma\beta}\bigr)P_{\nu^{\prime}}\overline{\psi^{(1)}}-\partial_{\alpha}\bigl(\widetilde{P}_{\nu,\beta}\delta\lambda_{\beta\gamma}\bigr)P_{\nu^{\prime}}\overline{(\lambda^{(1)})^{\alpha}_{\sigma}}\right]

\displaystyle+\nu^{-1}P_{\mu}L\left(P_{\nu}\left[(u_{3}^{(1)},A^{(1)})\delta\lambda+(\delta u_{3},\delta A)\lambda^{(2)}+\delta h\,\partial_{x}\lambda^{(2)}\right],P_{\nu^{\prime}}\lambda^{(1)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mu}{\nu}P_{\mu}L\bigl(P_{\nu}\delta\lambda,P_{\nu^{\prime}}\lambda^{(1)}\bigr)

\displaystyle+\nu^{-1}P_{\mu}L\left(P_{\nu}\left[(u_{3}^{(1)},A^{(1)})\delta\lambda+(\delta u_{3},\delta A)\lambda^{(2)}+\delta h\,\partial_{x}\lambda^{(2)}\right],P_{\nu^{\prime}}\lambda^{(1)}\right)

\displaystyle+\nu^{-1}P_{\mu}L\left(P_{\nu}\delta\lambda,P_{\nu^{\prime}}\left[(u_{3}^{(1)},A^{(1)})\lambda^{(1)}\right]\right).

(9.29)

Similarly, we can use the ordinary Codazzi equations for

\lambda^{(2)}
to obtain

\displaystyle P_{\mu}\left[(P_{\nu}\lambda_{\gamma\sigma}^{(2)})(P_{\nu^{\prime}}\overline{w})-(P_{\nu}\lambda_{\alpha\gamma}^{(2)})P_{\nu^{\prime}}\overline{\delta(\lambda^{\alpha}_{\sigma})}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mu}{\nu}P_{\mu}L\bigl(P_{\nu}\lambda^{(2)},P_{\nu^{\prime}}\delta\lambda\bigr)

\displaystyle+\nu^{-1}P_{\mu}L\left(P_{\nu}\left[(u_{3}^{(2)},A^{(2)})\lambda^{(2)}\right],P_{\nu^{\prime}}\delta\lambda\right)

(9.30)

\displaystyle+\nu^{-1}P_{\mu}L\left(P_{\nu}\lambda^{(2)},P_{\nu^{\prime}}\left[(u_{3}^{(1)},A^{(1)})\delta\lambda+(\delta u_{3},\delta A)\lambda^{(2)}+\delta h\,\partial_{x}\lambda^{(2)}\right]\right),

Combining
(
9.26
),
(
9.29
), and
(
9.30
), we obtain

\displaystyle P_{\mu}\delta\bigl(\lambda_{\nu}\wedge\lambda_{\nu^{\prime}}\bigr)=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mu}{\nu}P_{\mu}L\bigl(P_{\nu}\delta\lambda,P_{\nu^{\prime}}\lambda^{(1)}\bigr)+\frac{\mu}{\nu}P_{\mu}L\bigl(P_{\nu}\lambda^{(2)},P_{\nu^{\prime}}\delta\lambda\bigr)

(9.31)

\displaystyle+\nu^{-1}P_{\mu}L\left(P_{\nu}\left[(u_{3}^{(1)},A^{(1)})\delta\lambda+(\delta u_{3},\delta A)\lambda^{(2)}+\delta h\,\partial_{x}\lambda^{(2)}\right],P_{\nu^{\prime}}\lambda^{(1)}\right)

\displaystyle+\nu^{-1}P_{\mu}L\left(P_{\nu}\delta\lambda,P_{\nu^{\prime}}\left[(u_{3}^{(1)},A^{(1)})\lambda^{(1)}\right]\right)

\displaystyle+\nu^{-1}P_{\mu}L\left(P_{\nu}\left[(u_{3}^{(2)},A^{(2)})\lambda^{(2)}\right],P_{\nu^{\prime}}\delta\lambda\right)

\displaystyle+\nu^{-1}P_{\mu}L\left(P_{\nu}\lambda^{(2)},P_{\nu^{\prime}}\left[(u_{3}^{(1)},A^{(1)})\delta\lambda+(\delta u_{3},\delta A)\lambda^{(2)}+\delta h\,\partial_{x}\lambda^{(2)}\right]\right)

\displaystyle+P_{\mu}L\bigl(\delta h,\lambda^{(2)},\lambda^{(2)}\bigr).

Thus the two quadratic polarized interactions retain the crucial
output-frequency gain
\mu/\nu
, while every remaining term contains
one difference factor and at least two background factors.

By (
2.34
), we have

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{N}=

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{N}^{(1)}-\mathcal{N}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\delta\mathbf{U}\cdot\partial_{x}\psi^{(1)}+\mathbf{U}^{(2)}\cdot\partial_{x}w+wB^{(1)}+\psi^{(2)}\delta B

\displaystyle\quad+wu_{i}^{(1)}u_{j}^{(1)}+\psi^{(2)}\delta u_{i}u_{j}^{(1)}+\psi^{(2)}u_{i}^{(2)}\delta u_{j},

(9.32)

where
\mathbf{U}
is given in (
2.35
).
It suffices to consider the quadratic terms in (
9.32
), since the cubic terms can be handled by adapting the general argument in Remark
3.7
. Moreover, as in the treatment of (
9.14
), the terms
\mathbf{U}^{(2)}\cdot\partial_{x}w
and
wB^{(1)}
can be estimated by adapting the corresponding arguments in the proof of Theorem
1.3
.
For instance, by the fact
B^{(1)}
is real, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Im}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}P_{\rho}\bigl(wB^{(1)}\bigr)\overline{w}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s=\operatorname{Im}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}([P_{\rho},B^{(1)}]w)\overline{w}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s.

This has the same commutator structure as (
4.9
) and can therefore be controlled by the arguments used in (
4.10
)–(
4.12
).

For
\delta\mathbf{U}\cdot\partial_{x}\psi^{(1)}
, the Bony’s decomposition gives

\displaystyle P_{\rho}(\delta\mathbf{U}\cdot\partial_{x}\psi^{(1)})=

\displaystyle\,\delta\mathbf{U}_{\ll\rho}\cdot\partial_{x}\psi^{(1)}_{\rho}+\delta\mathbf{U}_{\rho}\cdot\partial_{x}\psi^{(1)}_{\ll\rho}

\displaystyle\,+P_{\rho}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\delta\mathbf{U}_{\nu}\cdot\partial_{x}\psi^{(1)}_{\nu^{\prime}}.

(9.33)

The high–low and high–high interactions can be handled by the standard
dyadic summation argument.
Indeed, the high–high contribution in (
9.33
) can be estimated in the same manner as (
9.20
).
While for the high–low contribution, Hölder’s inequality, the
(
9.7
), (
UA4
), and (
9.1
) imply

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\delta\mathbf{U}_{\rho}\cdot\partial_{x}\psi^{(1)}_{\ll\rho}\overline{w}_{\rho}dx\,ds\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|P_{\rho}\delta u_{\mathfrak{T}}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\cdot\big\|\|\partial_{x}P_{\ll\rho}\psi^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\widehat{x}_{\gamma}}}\|\overline{w}_{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{\gamma}}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{\gamma}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon D^{2}\rho^{-2s+2}d_{\rho}^{2},

for
\mathfrak{T}=4
or 5.
Indeed by (
9.7
), the index of low frequency
\mu
is positive and, hence the sum over
\mu\ll\rho
is dominated by its largest admissible frequency namely
\rho
, in accordance with the dyadic summation principle stated after Proposition
9.1
.

For the low–high interactions, we adapt the arguments used in the proof of Lemma
3.8
to close the estimates. As (
3.29
), we can use the decomposition (
2.1
) and only consider the case
\gamma=1
. Then, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}P_{\rho}\big(\delta\mathbf{U}_{\ll\rho}\partial_{x}\psi^{(1)}_{\rho}\big)\overline{w}_{\rho}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\rho\sup_{\tau}\left\|\|\delta\mathbf{U}_{\mu}\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\psi^{(1)}_{\sim\rho,1}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|w_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\right\|_{L^{1}_{t,y}}.

(9.34)

By (
2.35
),
we have

\displaystyle\Delta\delta\mathbf{U}=

\displaystyle\,h^{(1)}\cdot\partial_{x}^{2}\delta\mathbf{U}+\delta h\cdot\partial_{x}^{2}\mathbf{U}^{(2)}+u_{3}^{(1)}\partial_{x}\delta\mathbf{U}

\displaystyle\,+\delta u_{3}\partial_{x}\mathbf{U}^{(2)}+\partial_{x}\big(\delta(\lambda\wedge\lambda)\big)+\delta(u_{i}u_{j}u_{k}).

Hence, schematically,

\displaystyle P_{\mu}\delta\mathbf{U}

\displaystyle=L(h^{(1)}_{\ll\mu},P_{\mu}\delta\mathbf{U})+L(\delta h_{\ll\mu},P_{\mu}\mathbf{U}^{(2)})

\displaystyle\quad+\mu^{-1}L(P_{\mu}u_{3}^{(1)},P_{\ll\mu}\delta\mathbf{U})+\mu^{-1}L(P_{\mu}\delta u_{3},P_{\ll\mu}\mathbf{U}^{(2)})

(9.35)

\displaystyle\quad+\mu^{-2}\sum_{\rho\gg\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\mu}(P_{\mu}L(P_{\nu}\delta u_{3},P_{\nu^{\prime}}\partial_{x}u_{\mathfrak{T}}^{(2)})+P_{\mu}L(P_{\nu}u_{3}^{(1)},P_{\nu^{\prime}}\partial_{x}\delta u_{\mathfrak{T}}))

\displaystyle\quad+\mu^{-2}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}(P_{\mu}L(P_{\nu}\delta u_{3},P_{\nu^{\prime}}\partial_{x}u_{\mathfrak{T}}^{(2)})+P_{\mu}L(P_{\nu}u_{3}^{(1)},P_{\nu^{\prime}}\partial_{x}\delta u_{\mathfrak{T}}))

\displaystyle\quad+\mu^{-1}P_{\mu}\big(\delta(\lambda\wedge\lambda)\big)+\mu^{-2}P_{\mu}\delta(u_{i}u_{j}u_{k}).

The first term on the right-hand side of (
9.35
) is absorbed
by the smallness of
h^{(1)}
, while the cubic contributions are covered
by Remark
3.7
. For the second term,
(
9.8
) gives

\displaystyle\rho\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sup_{\tau}\left\|\|L(\delta h_{\ll\mu},P_{\mu}\mathbf{U}^{(2)})\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\psi_{\sim\rho,1}^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|w_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\right\|_{L^{1}_{t,y}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}D^{2}\rho^{-2s+2}d_{\rho}^{2}.

Similarly by (
9.9
), we can estimate the third and fourth term in (
9.35
).

The fifth and sixth term in (
9.35
) can be estimated in the same manner. We therefore estimate only the fifth term. The corresponding bilinear bounds yield

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\rho\gg\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\mu}\mu^{-2}\rho\sup_{\tau}\left\|\|P_{\mu}L(P_{\nu}\delta u_{3},P_{\nu^{\prime}}\partial_{x}u_{\mathfrak{T}}^{(2)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\psi_{\sim\rho,1}^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|w_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\right\|_{L^{1}_{t,y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\rho\gg\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\mu}\mu^{d-3}\rho\nu^{\prime}\big\|\|P_{\nu}\delta u_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\|\psi_{\sim\rho,1}^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{1}}}\big\|\|P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{\mathfrak{T}}^{(2)}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\|w_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{1}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{2}D^{2}\rho^{-2s+2}d_{\rho}^{2},

where
d\geq 5
.
Note the fact that scale-invariant total frequency power of the expression is
-2s+2
, the power of
\rho
is
-2s+1
, we know the sum of power of the low frequencies is
1
. The desired estimate then follows from the dyadic summation principle stated after Proposition
9.1
.

The seventh and eighth term in (
9.35
) can be estimated in the same manner and we will only estimate the seventh term as follows:

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\mu^{-2}\rho\sup_{\tau}\left\|\|P_{\mu}L(P_{\nu}\delta u_{3},P_{\nu^{\prime}}\partial_{x}u_{\mathfrak{T}}^{(2)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\psi_{\sim\rho,1}^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|w_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\right\|_{L^{1}_{t,y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\rho\mu^{d-2}\nu^{\prime}\left\|P_{\nu}\delta u_{3}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\left\|P_{\nu^{\prime}}u_{\mathfrak{T}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\cdot\left\|\psi_{\sim\rho}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\left\|w_{\rho}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\rho\mu^{d-2}\nu^{\prime}D\nu^{-s}d_{\nu}(\nu^{\prime})^{-s-1}c_{\nu^{\prime}}\rho^{-s}c_{\rho}D\rho^{-s+1}d_{\rho}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{2}D^{2}\rho^{-2s+2}d_{\rho}^{2}.

For the term
\mu^{-1}P_{\mu}\big(\delta(\lambda\wedge\lambda)\big)
, the Bony’s decomposition gives

\displaystyle P_{\mu}\big(\delta(\lambda\wedge\lambda)\big)=

\displaystyle L(P_{\ll\mu}\lambda^{(2)},P_{\mu}\delta\lambda)+L(P_{\mu}\lambda^{(2)},P_{\ll\mu}\delta\lambda)

(9.36)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\rho\gg\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\mu}P_{\mu}(L(\lambda_{\nu}^{(2)},\delta\lambda_{\nu^{\prime}})+\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}P_{\mu}\delta\bigl(\lambda_{\nu}\wedge\lambda_{\nu^{\prime}}\bigr).

The first three terms can be estimated by (
9.10
). For the
high–high contribution, it is enough to estimate the second quadratic term
in (
9.31
); the first one is identical, while the cubic
terms are covered by Remark
3.7
. Thus

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\mu^{-1}\frac{\mu}{\nu}\sup_{\tau}\left\|\|P_{\mu}L(P_{\nu}\lambda^{(2)},P_{\nu^{\prime}}\delta\lambda)\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\psi_{\sim\rho,1}^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|w_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\right\|_{L^{1}_{t,y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\rho\mu^{d-3}\nu^{-1}\|P_{\nu}\lambda^{(2)}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}\|P_{\nu^{\prime}}\delta\lambda\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}\left\|\psi_{\sim\rho,1}\right\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}\|w_{\rho,1}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\nu^{\prime}\sim\nu\gtrsim\rho}\sum_{\rho^{\prime}\sim\rho}\rho\mu^{d-3}\nu^{-1}\cdot\nu^{-s+\frac{1}{4}}c_{\nu}D(\nu^{\prime})^{-s+\frac{5}{4}}d_{\nu^{\prime}}\cdot(\rho^{\prime})^{-s+\frac{1}{4}}c_{\rho^{\prime}}\cdot D\rho^{-s+\frac{5}{4}}d_{\rho}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{2}D^{2}\rho^{-2s+2}d_{\rho}^{2},

where
d\geq 5.

This completes the estimate for the term

\delta\mathbf{U}\partial_{x}\psi^{(1)}
.

Next, we consider the term
\psi^{(2)}\delta B
in (
9.32
). Bony’s decomposition gives

P_{\rho}(\psi^{(2)}\delta B)=\psi^{(2)}_{\ll\rho}\delta B_{\rho}+\psi^{(2)}_{\rho}\delta B_{\ll\rho}+P_{\rho}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\psi^{(2)}_{\nu}\delta B_{\nu^{\prime}}.

For the low–high interaction, Hölder’s inequality and (
9.7
) yield

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int\psi^{(2)}_{\ll\rho}\delta B_{\rho}\overline{w}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\delta B_{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|\psi^{(2)}_{\ll\rho}w_{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon D^{2}\rho^{-2s+2}d^{2}_{\rho}

(9.37)

For the high–low interaction, Hölder’s inequality and (
9.6
) give

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int\psi^{(2)}_{\rho}\delta B_{\ll\rho}\overline{w}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\delta B_{\ll\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{t,y}L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\psi^{(2)}_{\rho}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}\|w_{\rho}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon D^{2}\rho^{-2s+2}d_{\rho}^{2}.

(9.38)

The high–high interaction can be estimated by the same dyadic summation argument used above:

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}P_{\rho}(\psi^{(2)}_{\nu}\delta B_{\nu^{\prime}})\overline{w}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\|\delta B_{\nu^{\prime}}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|\psi^{(2)}_{\nu}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}\|w_{\rho}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{\infty}_{z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon D^{2}\rho^{-2s+2}d_{\rho}^{2}.

This completes the estimate for the term

\psi^{(2)}\delta B
. Collecting the estimates obtained above, we conclude that

\displaystyle\eqref{UA1.term1}\lesssim\varepsilon D^{2}\rho^{-2s+2}d_{\rho}^{2}.

We now turn to (
9.13
), where
\partial_{\alpha}\big(\delta h^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\psi^{(2)}\big)
incurs an additional loss of one derivative and has no counterpart in the original equation (
1.16
). Consequently, some parts of the argument require the weaker estimate (
9.17
), rather than the stronger pointwise bound (
9.16
).
Integration by parts gives

\eqref{UA1.term2}=\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}P_{\rho}(\delta h^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\psi^{(2)})\cdot\partial_{\alpha}\overline{w}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\right|.

Bony’s decomposition yields

P_{\rho}(\delta h\,\partial_{x}\psi^{(2)})=\delta h_{\ll\rho}\partial_{x}\psi_{\rho}^{(2)}+\delta h_{\rho}\partial_{x}\psi_{\ll\rho}^{(2)}+\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}P_{\rho}L(\delta u_{3,\nu},\psi_{\nu^{\prime}}^{(2)}),

The high–low and high–high contributions can be estimated in the same manner as (
3.38
) and (
3.39
), respectively, using the corresponding weak energy, elliptic, and interaction Morawetz assumptions. More precisely,

\displaystyle\,\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big(\delta h_{\rho}^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\psi_{\ll\rho}^{(2)}\big)\partial_{\alpha}\overline{w}_{\rho}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|\delta u_{3,\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|\partial_{\beta}P_{\ll\rho}\psi^{(2)}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{\infty}_{z}}\|w_{\rho}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon D^{2}\rho^{-2s+2}d_{\rho}^{2}.

and

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}P_{\rho}L(\delta u_{3,\nu},\psi_{\nu^{\prime}}^{(2)})\partial_{\alpha}\overline{w}_{\rho}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\rho\|\delta u_{3,\nu}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|\psi_{\nu^{\prime}}^{(2)}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}\|w_{\rho}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{\infty}_{z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon D^{2}\rho^{-2s+2}d_{\rho}^{2}.

It remains to treat the low–high contribution. Decomposing the low frequency coefficient dyadically gives

\displaystyle\rho\int_{0}^{t}\int L(\delta h_{\ll\rho}^{\alpha\beta},\psi_{\rho}^{(2)})\partial_{\alpha}\overline{w}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\rho\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\int_{0}^{t}\int L(\delta h_{\mu}^{\alpha\beta},\psi_{\rho}^{(2)})\partial_{\alpha}\overline{w}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s.

(9.39)

As in the low–high analysis of (
9.33
), we use the elliptic
equation for
\delta h
to recover the apparently lost derivative.
Taking the difference of the schematic metric equation (
2.22
), we
obtain

\displaystyle\Delta\delta h

\displaystyle=h^{(1)}\partial_{x}^{2}\delta h+\delta h\partial_{x}^{2}h^{(2)}+(u_{3}^{(1)}+u_{3}^{(2)})\delta u_{3}+\delta(\lambda\wedge\lambda)+\delta(h\cdot\partial_{x}h\cdot\partial_{x}h)

Applying
P_{\mu}\Delta^{-1}
, we get

\displaystyle\delta h_{\mu}=

\displaystyle\,L(h^{(1)}_{\ll\mu},\delta h_{\mu})+L(\delta h_{\ll\mu},h^{(2)}_{\mu})+\mu^{-1}L(u_{3,\ll\mu}^{(a)},\delta h_{\mu})

(9.40)

\displaystyle\,+\mu^{-2}L(h^{(1)}_{\mu},P_{\ll\mu}\partial_{x}^{2}\delta h)+\mu^{-2}L(\delta h_{\mu},\partial_{x}^{2}h^{(2)}_{\ll\mu})+\mu^{-1}L(h_{\mu}^{(a)},\delta u_{3,\ll\mu})

\displaystyle+\mu^{-2}\sum_{\mu\lesssim\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\ll\rho}P_{\mu}L(u^{(a)}_{3,\nu},\delta u_{3,\nu^{\prime}})+\mu^{-2}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}P_{\mu}L(u^{(a)}_{3,\nu},\delta u_{3,\nu^{\prime}})

\displaystyle+\delta(\lambda\wedge\lambda)+\delta(h\cdot\partial_{x}h\cdot\partial_{x}h),

where
a=1,2
.
The contribution of
L(h^{(1)}_{\ll\mu},P_{\mu}\delta h)
can be absorbed, since

\|h^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\varepsilon.

The cubic terms can be handled by the general argument in Remark
3.7
and will therefore be omitted.

The second through sixth terms on the right-hand side of (
9.40
) are
handled similarly by (
9.8
)–(
9.10
).
We illustrate the argument with the third term. Substituting it into
(
9.39
) and applying (
9.9
), we obtain

\displaystyle\,\rho\mu^{-1}\left|\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\int_{0}^{t}\int L(u^{(a)}_{3,\ll\mu},\delta h_{\mu},\psi_{\rho}^{(2)})\partial_{\alpha}\overline{w}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\right|

(9.41)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\rho^{2}\mu^{-1}\big\|\|L(\delta h_{\mu},u^{(a)}_{3,\ll\mu})\|_{L^{\infty}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\|w_{\rho,1}^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\|\psi_{\sim\rho,1}^{(2)}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{1}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,D^{2}\rho^{-2s+2}c_{\rho}d_{\rho}\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}c_{\mu}d_{\mu},

which is of the form (
9.17
). Similarly, the second term on the right-hand side of (
9.40
) is
controlled by (
9.8
):

\displaystyle\,\rho\left|\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\int_{0}^{t}\int L(\delta h_{\ll\mu},h^{(2)}_{\mu},\psi_{\rho}^{(2)})\partial_{\alpha}\overline{w}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,D^{2}\rho^{-2s+2}c_{\rho}d_{\rho}\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}c_{\mu}d_{\mu}.

The seventh term on the right-hand side of (
9.40
) is estimated as
follows:

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\mu\lesssim\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\ll\rho}\rho\mu^{-2}\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int L(P_{\mu}(u_{3,\nu}^{(a)},\delta u_{3,\nu^{\prime}}),\psi_{\rho}^{(2)})\partial_{\alpha}\overline{w}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\mu\lesssim\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\ll\rho}\rho\mu^{-2}\sup_{\tau,\tau^{\prime}}\big\|\|P_{\mu}(u_{3,\nu}^{(a)},\delta u_{3,\nu^{\prime}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\|w_{\rho,1}^{\tau^{\prime}}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\|(\psi_{\sim\rho,1}^{(2)})^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{1}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\mu\lesssim\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\ll\rho}\rho\mu^{d-3}\sup_{\tau,\tau^{\prime}}\big\|\|P_{\mu}(u_{3,\nu}^{(a)},\delta u_{3,\nu^{\prime}})\|_{L^{1}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\|w_{\rho,1}^{\tau^{\prime}}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\|(\psi_{\sim\rho,1}^{(2)})^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x_{1}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\mu\lesssim\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\ll\rho}\rho\mu^{d-3}\nu^{-s}c_{\nu}D\rho^{-s+\frac{1}{2}}d_{\rho}\cdot(\nu^{\prime})^{-s+1}Dd_{\nu^{\prime}}\rho^{-s-\frac{1}{2}}c_{\rho}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,D^{2}\rho^{-2s+2}c_{\rho}d_{\rho}\sum_{\nu\ll\rho}c_{\nu}d_{\nu}.

For the eighth term on the right-hand side of (
9.40
), we have

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\rho\mu^{-2}\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int L(P_{\mu}(u_{3,\nu}^{(a)},\delta u_{3,\nu^{\prime}}),\psi_{\rho}^{(2)})\partial_{\alpha}\overline{w}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\right|

(9.42)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\rho\mu^{-2}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\|P_{\mu}(u_{3,\nu}^{(a)},\delta u_{3,\nu^{\prime}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{1}_{t}}\|\psi_{\rho}^{(2)}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\partial_{\alpha}\overline{w}_{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\rho^{2}\mu^{d-2}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\|\delta u_{3,\nu}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|u^{(2)}_{3,\nu^{\prime}}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|\psi_{\rho}^{(2)}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\overline{w}_{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{2}D^{2}\rho^{-2s+2}d_{\rho}^{2}.

It remains to estimate

\delta(\lambda\wedge\lambda)
in (
9.40
), whose Bony
decomposition is given in (
9.36
). When substituted into
(
9.39
), the first three terms in
(
9.36
) are controlled by (
9.10
), exactly
as for the second through seventh terms on the right-hand side of
(
9.40
).

For the fourth term in (
9.36
), we exploit the Gauss
structure in (
9.31
). It suffices to estimate the
second quadratic contribution, since the first is identical:

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\rho\mu^{-2}\frac{\mu}{\nu}\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int L(P_{\mu}(\lambda_{\nu},\delta\lambda_{\nu^{\prime}}),\psi_{\rho}^{(2)})\partial_{\alpha}\overline{w}_{\rho}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\right|

(9.43)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\rho^{2}\mu^{-1}\nu^{-1}\|P_{\mu}(\lambda_{\nu},\delta\lambda_{\nu^{\prime}})\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\left\|\psi_{\rho}\right\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}\|w_{\rho}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\rho^{2}\mu^{d-4}\nu^{-1}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\mu}\|\lambda_{\nu}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}\|\delta\lambda_{\nu^{\prime}}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}\left\|\psi_{\rho}\right\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}\|w_{\rho}\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\ll\rho}\sum_{\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}\gtrsim\rho}\rho^{2}\mu^{d-4}\nu^{-s-\frac{3}{4}}c_{\nu}\cdot D(\nu^{\prime})^{-s+\frac{5}{4}}d_{\nu^{\prime}}\cdot\rho^{-s+\frac{1}{4}}c_{\rho}\cdot D\rho^{-s+\frac{5}{4}}d_{\rho}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{2}D^{2}\rho^{-2s+2}d_{\rho}^{2},

provided that
d\geq 5
.

Finally, combining the estimates for
(
9.12
)–(
9.14
), we obtain (
9.15
).

9.3.
Difference interaction Morawetz and bilinear estimates

The difference interaction Morawetz estimate

\|w_{\rho}\|_{L_{t,y}^{4}L_{z}^{2}(I)}\lesssim\rho^{-s+\frac{5}{4}}d_{\rho}\bigl(D(0)+\varepsilon D(I)\bigr)

follows by adapting the argument of Section
6
to the
frequency localized difference equation

\displaystyle\sqrt{-1}\partial_{t}w_{\rho}+\partial_{\alpha}\bigl(g_{\ll\rho}^{\alpha\beta,(1)}\partial_{\beta}w_{\rho}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{N}_{\rho}-\partial_{\alpha}\bigl(g_{\gtrsim\rho}^{\alpha\beta,(1)}\partial_{\beta}w_{\rho}\bigr):=\mathcal{I}_{\rho}^{h,(1)}.

Indeed, repeating the argument of Appendix
A
, with

\psi_{\rho},\,g,\,h,\,\mathcal{I}_{\rho}^{h}

in (
6.3
)–(
6.10
) replaced respectively by

w_{\rho},\,g^{(1)},\,h^{(1)},\,\mathcal{I}_{\rho}^{h,(1)},
yields the corresponding interaction Morawetz inequality for
w_{\rho}
. The resulting terms are controlled by the estimates
established in Section
6
and
Subsection
9.2
, which yields the desired bound. We omit
the details.

We next turn to the difference bilinear estimates. We shall establish the following two types of estimates:

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau}\left\|\|w_{\mu}\|_{L_{\widehat{x}_{\gamma}}^{2}}\|\psi^{(a),\tau}_{\rho,\gamma}\|_{L_{\widehat{x}_{\gamma}}^{2}}\right\|_{L_{t,x_{\gamma}}^{2}(I)}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\mu^{-s+1}d_{\mu}\rho^{-s-\frac{1}{2}}c_{\rho}\left(D(0)+\varepsilon D(I)\right),

(9.44)

and

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau}\left\|\|\psi_{\mu}^{(a)}\|_{L_{\widehat{x}_{\gamma}}^{2}}\|w^{\tau}_{\rho,\gamma}\|_{L_{\widehat{x}_{\gamma}}^{2}}\right\|_{L_{t,x_{\gamma}}^{2}(I)}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\mu^{-s}c_{\mu}\rho^{-s+\frac{1}{2}}d_{\rho}\left(D(0)+\varepsilon D(I)\right),

(9.45)

where
a=1,2;\gamma=1,\cdots,d.
As in the proof of the a priori bilinear estimates in
Section
5
, our strategy is to reduce the nonlinear error terms
arising from the div–curl estimates to those already controlled by
the difference energy estimates in Subsection
9.2
or by
the corresponding a priori estimates in Section
5
.

We first prove (
9.44
). As in (
5.2
), the
frequency localized equation for
\psi_{\rho,1}^{(a)}
is

\displaystyle\sqrt{-1}\partial_{t}\psi^{(a)}_{\rho,1}+\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{d}\partial_{\alpha}(g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\psi^{(a)}_{\rho,1})=P_{\rho,1}\mathcal{N}^{(a)}-\partial_{\alpha}([P_{\rho,1},h^{\alpha\beta}]\partial_{\beta}\psi^{(a)}):=\mathcal{G}^{(a)}_{\rho},

Replacing
\rho
by
\mu
in (
9.11
), we similarly obtain

\displaystyle\sqrt{-1}\partial_{t}w_{\mu}+\partial_{\alpha}\big(g^{\alpha\beta}_{1}\partial_{\beta}w_{\mu}\big)=\mathfrak{N}_{\mu},

As in Section
5
, applying Lemma
2.2
to the momentum balance law for
\psi_{\rho,1}^{(a)}
and the mass balance
law for
w_{\mu}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\rho^{2}\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|w_{\mu}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\psi_{\rho,1}^{(a)}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t

(9.46)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|P_{\mu}w|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Re}(\partial_{1}\psi_{\rho}^{(a)}g^{1\beta}\partial_{\beta}\overline{\psi}_{\rho}^{(a)})\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Re}(P_{\mu}w\partial_{1}P_{\mu}\overline{w})\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Re}(\psi_{\rho}^{(a)}\,g^{1\beta}\partial_{\beta}\overline{\psi}_{\rho}^{(a)})\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle-\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{\rho}^{(a)}\partial_{1}\overline{\psi}_{\rho}^{(a)})\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Im}(P_{\mu}\overline{w}g^{1\beta}\partial_{\beta}P_{\mu}w)\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\rho\max_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\psi_{\rho}^{(a)}(t,\cdot)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\max_{0\leq t\leq T}\|w_{\mu}(t,\cdot)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}

(9.47)

\displaystyle\,+\Big|\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\left(\int_{-\infty}^{x_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Im}\left(\psi_{\rho,1}^{(a)}\partial_{1}\overline{\psi}_{\rho,1}^{(a)}\right)\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}y\right)

(9.48)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Im}\left(\overline{w}_{\mu}\mathfrak{N}_{\mu}\right)\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\,+\Big|\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\left(\int^{+\infty}_{x_{1}}\left\|w_{\mu}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\mathrm{d}y\right)

(9.49)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Re}\left(\partial_{\alpha}\psi_{\rho,1}^{(a)}\partial_{1}h^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\overline{\psi}^{(a)}_{\rho,1}\right)\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\,+\Big|\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\left(\int^{+\infty}_{x_{1}}\left\|w_{\mu}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\mathrm{d}y\right)\Big|

(9.50)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Re}\left(\overline{\psi}_{\rho,1}^{(a)}\partial_{1}\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{(a)}-\mathcal{G}_{\rho}^{(a)}\partial_{1}\overline{\psi}_{\rho,1}^{(a)}\right)\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{2}\rho^{-2s+1}\mu^{-2s+2}c_{\rho}^{2}d_{\mu}^{2}(D^{2}(0)+D^{2}(I)).

Indeed, for the first nonlinear contribution, we have

\displaystyle\eqref{non.uni.bit1}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\rho\left\|\psi_{\rho}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\left\|\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathfrak{N}_{\mu}\overline{w}_{\mu}\right)\right\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}},

where the estimates

\left\|\operatorname{Im}\left(\mathfrak{N}_{\mu}\overline{w_{\mu}}\right)\right\|_{L_{t,x}^{1}}\lesssim\varepsilon D^{2}\mu^{-2s+2}d_{\mu}^{2}

has already been obtained in Subsection
9.2
. The
nonlinear contributions in (
9.49
) and
(
9.50
) can be estimated by adapting the corresponding
arguments from the proof of the a priori bilinear estimates in
Section
5
. This proves (
9.44
).

Similarly, for (
9.45
), we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{-1}\partial_{t}\psi^{(a)}_{\mu}+\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{d}\partial_{\alpha}(g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\psi^{(a)}_{\mu})=P_{\mu}\mathcal{N}-\partial_{\alpha}([P_{\mu},h^{\alpha\beta}]\partial_{\beta}\psi^{(a)}):=\mathcal{I}_{\mu}^{(a)}

and

\displaystyle\sqrt{-1}\partial_{t}w_{\rho,1}+\partial_{\alpha}\big(g^{\alpha\beta}_{1}\partial_{\beta}w_{\rho,1}\big)=\mathfrak{N}_{\rho},

where
\mathfrak{N}_{\rho}
is given in (
9.11
).
Thus the same process as those in Section
5
, we have

\displaystyle\rho^{2}\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\psi_{\mu}^{(a)}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|w_{\rho,1}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\rho\max_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\psi_{\mu}^{(a)}(t,\cdot)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\max_{0\leq t\leq T}\|w_{\rho}(t,\cdot)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}

(9.51)

\displaystyle\,+\Big|\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\left(\int_{-\infty}^{x_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Im}\left(w_{\rho,1}\partial_{1}\overline{w}_{\rho,1}\right)\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}y\right)

(9.52)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Im}\left(\overline{\psi}_{\mu}\mathcal{I}_{\mu}^{(a)}\right)\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\,+\Big|\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\left(\int^{+\infty}_{x_{1}}\big\|\psi_{\mu}^{(a)}\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\mathrm{d}y\right)

(9.53)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Re}\left(\partial_{\alpha}w_{\rho,1}\partial_{1}h^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\overline{w}_{\rho,1}\right)\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\,+\Big|\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\left(\int^{+\infty}_{x_{1}}\big\|\psi_{\mu}^{(a)}\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\mathrm{d}y\right)\Big|

(9.54)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathrm{Re}\left(\overline{w}_{\rho,1}\partial_{1}\mathfrak{N}_{\rho}-\mathfrak{N}_{\rho}\partial_{1}\overline{w}_{\rho,1}\right)\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}t\Big|.

The (
9.52
) and (
9.53
) can be estimated similar to (
5.8
) and (
5.9
) respectively. While by (
9.32
), we can use the same analysis for (
2.41
) to obtain

\displaystyle\eqref{1non.uni.bit3}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\big\|\psi_{\mu}^{(a)}\big\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\cdot\Big(\rho\,\|L(w_{\rho,1},P_{\rho}\delta\mathcal{N}-wB^{(1)}+[P_{\rho,1},B^{(1)}]w)\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{x}w_{\rho,1}\cdot P_{\rho}\partial_{\alpha}\big(\delta h^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\psi^{(2)}\big)\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}

(9.55)

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{x}w_{\rho,1}\cdot\partial_{\alpha}\big([P_{\rho},h^{\alpha\beta,(1)}]\partial_{\beta}w\big)\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}\Big)

\displaystyle\,+\Big|\int_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\left(\int^{+\infty}_{x_{1}}\big\|\psi_{\mu}^{(a)}\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\widehat{x}_{1}}}\mathrm{d}y\right)

(9.56)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\partial_{1}\big(w_{\rho}\cdot([P_{\rho,1},B^{(1)}]w)\big)-\partial_{1}B^{(1)}\cdot|w_{\rho,1}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\widehat{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\,\mathrm{d}t\Big|.

The (
9.55
) can be estimated as those in Subsection
3.1
, while (
9.56
) can be estimated similar to (
5.12
). This proves (
9.45
).

9.4.
Difference Elliptic and
\delta B
Estimates

Unlike the difference Schrödinger equation (
9.11
), the difference elliptic equations and the difference
B
-equation, derived respectively from (
2.30
), (
2.31
), and (
2.32
), contain no derivative-losing term analogous to
P_{\rho}\partial_{\alpha}\bigl(\delta h^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\psi^{(2)}\bigr).
Consequently, the estimates for the difference elliptic variables
\delta u_{i}
,
i=2,3,4,5
, and for
\delta B
follow by adapting the corresponding arguments in Sections
7
and
8
. We therefore omit the details.

Combining the estimates established in Subsections
9.2
–
9.4
, we complete the proof of Proposition
9.1
.

9.5.
Proof of Theorem
1.1

Let
\mathcal{J}_{1/n}
be a standard smooth Fourier multiplier and define

\psi_{0}^{n}=\mathcal{J}_{1/n}\psi_{0}.

Then

\|\psi_{0}^{n}\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}\lesssim\|\psi_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}},

and
\psi_{0}^{n}\longrightarrow\psi_{0}~~\text{in }\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}.

Moreover, for every integer
m\geq 0
,

\|\psi_{0}^{n}\|_{\dot{B}^{s+m}_{2,1}}\lesssim n^{m}\|\psi_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}.

For each
n
, let

\mathfrak{S}_{0}^{n}:=\mathfrak{S}(\psi_{0}^{n})=\left(\lambda_{0}^{n},h_{0}^{n},V_{0}^{n},A_{0}^{n},B_{0}^{n}\right)

be the solution of the elliptic constraint system in the harmonic/Coulomb
gauge. Then

(\psi_{0}^{n},\mathfrak{S}_{0}^{n})

is a smooth compatible datum. By the high regularity local well-posedness
theory in Huang–Tataru
[
14
]
, there exists a maximal high regularity solution

\left(\psi^{n},\lambda^{n},h^{n},V^{n},A^{n},B^{n}\right)

on a maximal interval
[0,T_{n}^{*}).

We claim that

T_{n}^{*}=\infty.

Indeed, the Theorem
1.3
gives

\displaystyle\sup_{t<T_{n}^{*}}\sum_{i=1}^{5}\|u_{i}^{n}(t)\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\sup_{t<T_{n}^{*}}\|u_{i}^{n}(t)\|_{\dot{B}^{s+2}_{2,1}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{t<T_{n}^{*}}\sum_{\rho}\rho^{s+2}\|P_{\rho}u_{i}^{n}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\psi_{0}^{n}\|_{\dot{B}^{s+2}_{2,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{2}\|\psi_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}=:C_{n}<\infty.

(9.57)

The constant
C_{n}
may depend on
n
, but it is uniform in

t<T_{n}^{*}
. Furthermore, since

\|h^{n}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\infty}([0,T_{n}^{*}))}\lesssim\varepsilon,

the metric

g_{n}=I+h^{n}

remains uniformly elliptic. The continuation criterion for high regularity
solutions therefore excludes a finite maximal time. Hence

T_{n}^{*}=\infty.

We have constructed a sequence of global smooth compatible solutions
satisfying

\sup_{n}\sup_{t\geq 0}\sum_{i=1}^{5}\|u_{i}^{n}(t)\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}\lesssim\varepsilon,

together with the uniform energy, bilinear, interaction Morawetz, elliptic and

B
estimates of Theorem
1.3
.

Fix
T>0
. For
n,m\geq 1
, set

w^{n,m}=\psi^{n}-\psi^{m}.

Applying Proposition
9.1
on
[0,T]
, we
obtain

\|\psi^{n}-\psi^{m}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}^{s-1}}\lesssim\|\psi_{0}^{n}-\psi_{0}^{m}\|_{\dot{B}^{s-1}_{2,1}},

where
\mathcal{X}_{T}^{s-1}
denotes the collection of difference weak energy,
bilinear, interaction Morawetz, elliptic and
B
norms occurring in

D([0,T])
.

It is evident that the right-hand side tends to zero. Indeed, since

\widehat{\mathcal{J}}
is smooth and equals one at the origin, on the
support of
P_{\rho}
we have

\displaystyle\|\psi_{0}^{n}-\psi_{0}^{m}\|_{\dot{B}^{s-1}_{2,1}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left(\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{m}\right)\|\psi_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}

and hence
\{\psi^{n}\}
is Cauchy in
\mathcal{X}_{T}^{s-1}
.

There exists a limit
\psi
such that

\psi^{n}\longrightarrow\psi~~\text{in}\mathcal{X}_{T}^{s-1}.

The difference elliptic estimates imply, at the same time, that

\lambda^{n}\longrightarrow\lambda,\quad\partial_{x}h^{n}\longrightarrow\partial_{x}h,\quad V^{n}\longrightarrow V,\quad A^{n}\longrightarrow A

in the corresponding weak topologies, and

B^{n}\longrightarrow B

in the weak dyadic
B
topology.

We next show that the convergence is strong in

C([0,T];\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}).

Define

c_{\rho}=\sum_{\nu}2^{-\delta|\log_{2}(\rho/\nu)|}\nu^{s}\|P_{\nu}\psi_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{2}},

where
\delta>0
is sufficiently small. Then

\sum_{\rho}c_{\rho}\lesssim\|\psi_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}.

Since
\mathcal{J}_{1/n}
is a uniformly bounded Fourier multiplier, the
same envelope controls every approximate datum:

\rho^{s}\|P_{\rho}\psi_{0}^{n}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim c_{\rho}.

The frequency envelope version of Theorem
1.3
propagates this
bound, so that

\sup_{n}\rho^{s}\|P_{\rho}\psi^{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}([0,T])}\lesssim c_{\rho}.

Passing to the weak limit at each fixed frequency gives

\rho^{s}\|P_{\rho}\psi\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}([0,T])}\lesssim c_{\rho}.

Fix a dyadic number
R>1
and decompose

\psi^{n}-\psi=P_{\leq R}(\psi^{n}-\psi)+P_{>R}(\psi^{n}-\psi).

For the low-frequency part,

\displaystyle\|P_{\leq R}(\psi^{n}-\psi)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}([0,T])}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\rho\leq R}\rho^{s}\|P_{\rho}(\psi^{n}-\psi)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}([0,T])}

\displaystyle\leq R\sum_{\rho\leq R}\rho^{s-1}\|P_{\rho}(\psi^{n}-\psi)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}([0,T])}

\displaystyle\leq R\|\psi^{n}-\psi\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\dot{B}^{s-1}_{2,1}([0,T])}.

(9.58)

For fixed
R
, the last expression tends to zero as
n\to\infty
.

For the high-frequency part,

\displaystyle\|P_{>R}(\psi^{n}-\psi)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}([0,T])}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\rho>R}\rho^{s}\left(\|P_{\rho}\psi^{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}([0,T])}+\|P_{\rho}\psi\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}([0,T])}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\rho>R}c_{\rho}.

(9.59)

Since
\{c_{\rho}\}\in\ell^{1}
, the right-hand side tends to zero as

R\to\infty
, uniformly in
n
. We first choose
R
sufficiently large
and then let
n\to\infty
. It follows that

\psi^{n}\longrightarrow\psi\quad\text{in }C([0,T];\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}).

The critical elliptic stability estimates similarly imply

\lambda^{n}\longrightarrow\lambda,\quad\partial_{x}h^{n}\longrightarrow\partial_{x}h,\quad V^{n}\longrightarrow V,\quad A^{n}\longrightarrow A

in the corresponding critical Besov topologies. The variable
B^{n}

converges in the dyadic fixed-time and space–time topology associated with
the estimates for
B
.

It remains to show that the limit satisfies the full system. We write the
Schrödinger equation in the form

\sqrt{-1}\,\partial_{t}\psi^{n}+\Delta\psi^{n}=-\partial_{\alpha}\left(h_{n}^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\psi^{n}\right)+\mathcal{N}^{n}.

For the principal quasilinear term,

\displaystyle h_{n}^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\psi^{n}-h^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\psi=

\displaystyle\,\left(h_{n}^{\alpha\beta}-h^{\alpha\beta}\right)\partial_{\beta}\psi^{n}

\displaystyle+h^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}(\psi^{n}-\psi).

(9.60)

The mixed coefficient estimates used in the proof of
Proposition
9.1
show that the right-hand
side of (
9.60
) tends to zero in the
corresponding nonlinear distribution space.

For the lower-order terms, we use

u_{\mathfrak{T}}^{n}\partial_{x}\psi^{n}-u_{\mathfrak{T}}\partial_{x}\psi=(u_{\mathfrak{T}}^{n}-u_{\mathfrak{T}})\partial_{x}\psi^{n}+u_{\mathfrak{T}}\partial_{x}(\psi^{n}-\psi),

\displaystyle\psi^{n}u_{i}^{n}u_{j}^{n}-\psi u_{i}u_{j}=

\displaystyle\,(\psi^{n}-\psi)u_{i}^{n}u_{j}^{n}+\psi(u_{i}^{n}-u_{i})u_{j}^{n}

\displaystyle+\psi u_{i}(u_{j}^{n}-u_{j}),

(9.61)

and

B^{n}\psi^{n}-B\psi=(B^{n}-B)\psi^{n}+B(\psi^{n}-\psi).

The nonlinear estimates from the preceding sections, together with the
strong critical convergence and the uniform space–time bounds, imply that
all these differences tend to zero in distributions on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

The elliptic equations pass to the limit by the same argument. Therefore

\left(\psi,\lambda,h,V,A,B\right)

satisfies
(
1.16
)-(
1.18
)
in the distributional sense. Since

\psi^{n}(0)=\psi_{0}^{n}\longrightarrow\psi_{0}~~\text{in }\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1},

and

\psi^{n}\longrightarrow\psi~~\text{in }C([0,T];\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}),

we obtain

\psi(0)=\psi_{0}.

Since
T>0
was arbitrary and all a priori estimates are uniform in

T
, the same construction gives a global solution satisfying

\psi\in C([0,\infty);\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}),

and

\sup_{t\geq 0}\sum_{i=1}^{5}\|u_{i}(t)\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}\lesssim\varepsilon,

together with the global space–time, bilinear, interaction Morawetz, and

B
-estimates.

Now we prove uniqueness. Let

\left(\psi^{(a)},\lambda^{(a)},h^{(a)},V^{(a)},A^{(a)},B^{(a)}\right),\qquad a=1,2,

be two solutions in the critical solution class with the same initial datum:

\psi^{(1)}(0)=\psi^{(2)}(0)=\psi_{0}.

The weak difference estimate was proved first for smooth solutions. By
applying it to smooth approximations of the two solutions and passing to the
limit, it remains valid for critical solutions. Hence, on every finite
interval
I=[0,T]
,

D(I)\lesssim\|\psi^{(1)}(0)-\psi^{(2)}(0)\|_{\dot{B}^{s-1}_{2,1}}.

The right-hand side is zero. Therefore

D(I)=0.

In particular,

\psi^{(1)}=\psi^{(2)}~~\text{on }[0,T].

The elliptic difference estimates then imply

\lambda^{(1)}=\lambda^{(2)},~~h^{(1)}=h^{(2)},~~V^{(1)}=V^{(2)},~~A^{(1)}=A^{(2)},~~B^{(1)}=B^{(2)}.

Since
T>0
is arbitrary, the two global solutions coincide on

[0,\infty)
. This proves uniqueness. The continuous dependence on the initial data can be proved in the same manner.

This completes the proof of Theorem
1.1
.

9.6.
Proof of Corollary
1.2

We divide the proof into three steps: the construction of the initial harmonic/Coulomb gauge, the reconstruction of the geometric flow from the good gauge variables, and the application of Theorem
1.1
. We begin with the construction of harmonic coordinates at the initial time.

Proposition 9.5

(Critical Besov harmonic coordinates)
.

Let
d\geq 4
,
s=d/2-1
, and let
F:\mathbb{R}_{x}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d+2}
be an immersion with induced metric
g=\mathrm{Id}+h
. Assume

\|\nabla h\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}\leq\varepsilon,~~h(x)\to 0\quad(|x|\to\infty),

where
\varepsilon>0
is sufficiently small. Then there exists a unique change of coordinates

y=x+\varphi(x),~~\varphi(x)\to 0\quad(|x|\to\infty),

with
\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\ll 1
, such that the transformed metric
\widetilde{g}
satisfies

\widetilde{g}^{\alpha\beta}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\gamma}_{\alpha\beta}=0
. Moreover,

\|\nabla^{2}\varphi\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}\lesssim\|\nabla h\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}},~~\|\nabla_{y}(\widetilde{g}-\mathrm{Id})\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}\lesssim\|\nabla_{x}(g-\mathrm{Id})\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}.

If, in addition,
\|H\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}\leq\varepsilon
, then, for
\widetilde{F}(y)=F(x(y))
,

\|\nabla_{y}^{2}\widetilde{F}\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}\lesssim\|\nabla h\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}+\|H\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}.

Proof.

We follow the harmonic coordinate construction of Huang–Tataru
[
14
, Proposition 8.2]
, replacing the Sobolev estimates there by the critical Besov embedding

\dot{B}^{d/2}_{2,1}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}
and the corresponding paraproduct estimates. Writing
y^{\gamma}=x^{\gamma}+\varphi^{\gamma}
and imposing
\Delta_{g}y^{\gamma}=0
gives schematically

\Delta\varphi=\partial h+Q(h,\partial h)+Q(h,\partial^{2}\varphi)+Q(\partial h,\partial\varphi).

Hence Riesz-transform bounds and critical Besov product estimates yield

\|\nabla^{2}\varphi\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}\lesssim\|\nabla h\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}+\varepsilon\|\nabla^{2}\varphi\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}.

The last term is absorbed for small
\varepsilon
, and a contraction argument gives existence and uniqueness. The resulting map is bi-Lipschitz because

\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|\nabla^{2}\varphi\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}
; standard paracomposition estimates then give the bound for
\widetilde{g}
.

The key geometric structure is that in harmonic coordinates the metric equation is genuinely elliptic,

\widetilde{g}^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha\beta}\widetilde{h}=Q(\partial\widetilde{h},\partial\widetilde{h})+Q(\widetilde{\lambda},\widetilde{\lambda}),

where the Gauss equation has been used and no second derivative occurs on the right-hand side. Likewise,

\widetilde{g}^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha\beta}\widetilde{F}=H
.
Perturbative elliptic estimates therefore give the asserted bound for
\nabla_{y}^{2}\widetilde{F}
.
∎

Having fixed harmonic coordinates, we next impose the Coulomb condition on
the normal frame. As in Huang–Tataru
[
14
]
, we rotate an
asymptotically constant normal frame according to
m^{\prime}=e^{i\theta}m
and solve

\Delta_{g}\theta=\nabla^{\alpha}A_{\alpha}
, with
\theta(\infty)=0
. The same critical Besov elliptic estimates give

\|\nabla\theta\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}\lesssim\|A\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}
.
Thus the harmonic coordinate and Coulombframe constructions place the
initial immersion in the good gauge. In particular, the resulting initial
complex mean curvature satisfies

\|\psi_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}\lesssim\|\nabla(g_{0}-\mathrm{Id})\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}+\|H_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}\lesssim\varepsilon.

We now explain how to reconstruct the geometric flow from the global
good gauge solution.
Let
(\psi,\lambda,h,V,A,B)
be the global good gauge solution. The reconstruction is algebraically identical to Section 8 of Huang–Tataru
[
14
]
. One solves the spatial and temporal frame equations

\partial_{\alpha}F_{\beta}=\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\alpha\beta}F_{\gamma}+\operatorname{Re}(\lambda_{\alpha\beta}\bar{m}),\qquad\partial_{\alpha}^{A}m=-\lambda_{\alpha}^{\gamma}F_{\gamma},

\partial_{t}F_{\alpha}=-\operatorname{Im}\!\left((\partial_{\alpha}^{A}\psi-i\lambda_{\alpha\gamma}V^{\gamma})\bar{m}\right)+\left(\operatorname{Im}(\psi\bar{\lambda}_{\alpha}^{\gamma})+\nabla_{\alpha}V^{\gamma}\right)F_{\gamma},

with the analogous equation for
\partial_{t}^{B}m
.

The Gauss–Codazzi–Ricci identities provide the compatibility conditions
for the spatial frame equations, while the evolution equations for

g
,
\lambda
, and
A
provide the space–time compatibility
conditions. Frobenius’ theorem therefore yields the moving frame

(F_{1},\ldots,F_{d},m).

The immersion is then recovered by integrating

\partial_{\alpha}F=F_{\alpha}.

By construction,

\partial_{t}F=-\operatorname{Im}(\psi\overline{m})+V^{\gamma}F_{\gamma},

and hence

(\partial_{t}F)^{\perp}=-\operatorname{Im}(\psi\overline{m})=JH.

Therefore
F
solves the skew mean curvature flow (
1.2
).

The geometric reconstruction is the same as in
Huang–Tataru
[
14
]
; the only difference is analytic. Here the
good gauge solution is global and is constructed at the critical Besov
regularity. To justify the reconstruction at this regularity, we first
apply the preceding argument to the smooth approximate solutions. The
critical a priori estimates and the weak difference estimates allow us to
pass to the limit in the frame equations, the constraint identities, and
the evolution equations. Consequently, the limiting immersion satisfies
the same geometric identities and solves (
1.2
) globally.

We can now complete the proof. By
Proposition
9.5
and the Coulomb
frame construction, the initial immersion can be placed in the good gauge,
with

\|\psi_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,1}}\lesssim\varepsilon.

Theorem
1.1
then yields a unique global solution of the
Schrödinger–elliptic system satisfying all constraint and evolution
identities. The reconstruction argument above produces a corresponding
global solution of the skew mean curvature flow. Uniqueness and continuous
dependence follow from the corresponding properties of the good gauge
system once the normalization at infinity, and hence the residual
rigid-motion freedom, has been fixed. This completes the proof of
Corollary
1.2
.

Appendix A
Interaction Morawetz Estimates

In this appendix, we establish interaction Morawetz estimates for the following quasilinear Schrödinger equation:

\begin{cases}\sqrt{-1}u_{t}+\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{d}\partial_{\alpha}(g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}u)=\mathcal{N}(u),\qquad x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\ (d\geq 3),\\
u|_{t=0}=u_{0},\end{cases}

(QNLS)

where the metric
g
satisfies the evolution equation

\partial_{t}g^{\alpha\beta}=H^{\alpha\beta}

(A.1)

and we denote
g^{\alpha\beta}=\delta_{\alpha\beta}+h^{\alpha\beta}
, with
h^{\alpha\beta}
satisfying

\|h^{\alpha\beta}\|_{L^{\infty}}\ll 1.

We begin by introducing some notations. Let
\partial^{\alpha}:=g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}
and

x=(y,z)=(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},z_{4},\dots,z_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d},y\in\mathbb{R}^{3},z\in\mathbb{R}^{d-3}.

For simplicity, we limit the Roman indices
i,j,k,l
to range from
1
to
3
, corresponding to the variables
y=(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}),y^{\prime}=(y_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}^{\prime},y_{3}^{\prime})
.

The mass balance law for (
QNLS
) is

\displaystyle\,\partial_{t}\left|u\right|^{2}+\partial_{\alpha}\mathrm{Im}(\overline{u}\partial^{\alpha}u)=\mathrm{Im}\left(\overline{u}\mathcal{N}(u)\right),

(A.2)

while the momentum balance law reads

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathrm{Im}(\overline{u}\partial_{\gamma}u)+\partial_{\alpha}\mathrm{Re}(2\partial_{\gamma}u\partial^{\alpha}\overline{u}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\gamma}\partial^{\alpha}|u|^{2})

(A.3)

\displaystyle=-\partial_{\gamma}\mathrm{Re}(\bar{u}\mathcal{N}(u))+2\mathrm{Re}(\mathcal{N}(u)\partial_{\gamma}\overline{u})-\mathrm{Re}(\partial_{\alpha}u\partial_{\gamma}h^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\overline{u}).

Using (
A.1
), we raise the index
\gamma
in (
A.3
) and obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathrm{Im}(\overline{u}\partial^{\gamma}u)+\partial_{\alpha}\mathrm{Re}(2\partial^{\gamma}u\partial^{\alpha}\overline{u}-\frac{1}{2}\partial^{\gamma}\partial^{\alpha}|u|^{2})

(A.4)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Re}(\mathcal{N}(u)\partial^{\gamma}\overline{u}-\bar{u}\partial^{\gamma}\mathcal{N}(u))+H\cdot u\cdot\partial_{x}u+\partial_{x}h\cdot[(\partial_{x}u)^{2}+u\cdot\partial_{x}^{2}u]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}(u)+H\cdot u\cdot\partial_{x}u+\partial_{x}h\cdot[(\partial_{x}u)^{2}+u\cdot\partial_{x}^{2}u].

Let
a(y)=|y|
. Then there holds

\displaystyle a_{k}(y):=(\partial_{k}a)(y)=\frac{y_{k}}{|y|},

\displaystyle a_{kl}(y):=(\partial_{kl}a)(y)=\frac{\delta_{kl}}{|y|}-\frac{y_{k}y_{l}}{|y|^{3}},

\displaystyle(\Delta a)(y)=\frac{2}{|y|},

\displaystyle(\Delta^{2}a)(y)=-8\pi\delta(y).

Moreover, for
m\geq 1
, we can interpret
\partial^{m}_{y}a

as kernels for appropriate multipliers, which act like the composition of
|\nabla_{y}|^{4-m}
and Riesz type operators
R_{y}
.

We define the
spatially localized Morawetz interaction potential

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{d-3}\times\mathbb{R}^{d-3}}a_{k}(y-y^{\prime})|u(y,z)|^{2}\mathrm{Im}(\bar{u}\partial^{k}u)(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}

(A.5)

For convenience, we omit both the integration domain and the explicit volume elements in what follows. Unless otherwise stated, we set

(y,z),(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{d-3},

and it is understood that the variables
y,y^{\prime},z,z^{\prime}
implicitly include their corresponding measures
\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime},\mathrm{d}z,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}
, which are suppressed for brevity.

Differentiating the potential (
A.5
) in time and integrating by parts, we apply (
A.2
) and (
A.4
) to obtain

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int a_{k}(y-y^{\prime})|u(y,z)|^{2}\mathrm{Im}(\bar{u}\partial^{k}u)(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})\,=\mathrm{J}(u)+\text{Err}_{1}+\text{Err}_{2},

(A.6)

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{J}(u):=

\displaystyle\,\int a_{kl}(y-y^{\prime})\,|u(y,z)|^{2}\,[2\mathrm{Re}(\partial^{k}\overline{u}\partial^{l}u)-\frac{1}{2}\partial^{k}\partial^{l}|u|^{2}](y^{\prime},z^{\prime})

\displaystyle-\int a_{kl}(y-y^{\prime})\,2\mathrm{Im}(\overline{u}\partial^{l}u)(y,z)\mathrm{Im}(\overline{u}\partial^{k}u)(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})]\,

\displaystyle\text{Err}_{1}:=

\displaystyle\,-\int a_{k}(y-y^{\prime})\,\Big[|u(y,z)|^{2}\,\operatorname{Re}\big(\mathcal{N}(u)\partial^{k}\overline{u}-\bar{u}\partial^{k}\mathcal{N}(u)\big)(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\mathrm{Im}\left(\overline{u}\mathcal{N}(u)\right)(y,z)\,\mathrm{Im}(\overline{u}\partial^{k}u)(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})\Big]\,

\displaystyle\text{Err}_{2}:=

\displaystyle\,\int a_{k}(y-y^{\prime})\,|u(y,z)|^{2}\,\{H\cdot u\cdot\partial_{x}u+\partial_{x}h\cdot[(\partial_{x}u)^{2}+u\cdot\partial_{x}^{2}u]\}(y^{\prime},z^{\prime}).\,

We now simplify
\mathrm{J}(u)
. Integration by parts yields

\displaystyle-\int a_{kl}(y-y^{\prime})\,|u(y,z)|^{2}\,\frac{1}{2}\partial^{k}\partial^{l}|u|^{2}(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int a_{kl}(y-y^{\prime})\,\frac{1}{2}\partial^{k}|u(y,z)|^{2}\,\cdot\partial^{l}|u(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})|^{2}+\text{Err}_{3}

(A.7)

where

\displaystyle\text{Err}_{3}:=\int(\partial^{2}a)(y-y^{\prime})\big[(\partial_{x}h\cdot\partial_{x}|u|^{2})(y,z)\,|u|^{2}(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})+(\partial_{x}h\cdot|u|^{2})(y,z)\,\partial_{x}|u(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})|^{2}\big].

Observing the identity

\displaystyle\mathrm{Re}[(u(y,z)\partial^{k}u(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})-\partial^{k}u(y,z)u(y^{\prime},z^{\prime}))\cdot(\bar{u}(y,z)\partial^{l}\bar{u}(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})-\partial^{l}\bar{u}(y,z)\bar{u}(y^{\prime},z^{\prime}))]

\displaystyle=|u(y,z)|^{2}\mathrm{Re}(\partial^{k}\bar{u}\partial^{l}u)(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})+|u(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})|^{2}\mathrm{Re}(\partial^{k}\bar{u}\partial^{l}u)(y,z)

\displaystyle\quad-2\mathrm{Re}(\partial^{k}u(y,z)u(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})\cdot\bar{u}(y,z)\partial^{l}\bar{u}(y^{\prime},z^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=|u(y,z)|^{2}\mathrm{Re}(\partial^{k}\bar{u}\partial^{l}u)(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})+|u(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})|^{2}\mathrm{Re}(\partial^{k}\bar{u}\partial^{l}u)(y,z)

\displaystyle\quad-2\,\mathrm{Im}(\bar{u}\partial^{k}u)(y,z)\,\mathrm{Im}(\bar{u}\partial^{l}u)(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})-\frac{1}{2}\partial^{k}|u(y,z)|^{2}\partial^{l}|u(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})|^{2},

together with the symmetry of the kernel
a_{kl}
and the convexity of
a
, we use (
A.7
) to obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{J}(u)

\displaystyle=\int a_{kl}(y-y^{\prime})[F^{k}\bar{F}^{l}+\partial^{l}|u(y,z)|^{2}\partial^{l}|u(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})|^{2}]+\text{Err}_{3}

(A.8)

\displaystyle\geq\int a_{kl}(y-y^{\prime})\partial^{k}|u(x)|^{2}\partial^{l}|u(y)|^{2}+\text{Err}_{3},

where
F^{k}=u(y,z)\partial^{k}u(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})-\partial^{k}u(y,z)u(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})
.
The dominant term is handled by further integration by parts:

\displaystyle\int a_{kl}(y-y^{\prime})\partial^{l}|u(y,z)|^{2}\partial^{k}|u(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})|^{2}=8\pi\|u\|_{L^{4}_{y}L^{2}_{z}}^{4}+\text{Err}_{4},

(A.9)

with

\displaystyle\text{Err}_{4}:=

\displaystyle\,\int(\partial^{2}a)(y-y^{\prime})(\partial_{x}h\cdot|u|^{2})(y,z)\,\partial_{x}|u(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})|^{2}

\displaystyle+\int(\partial^{3}a)(y-y^{\prime})(\partial_{x}h\cdot|u|^{2})(y,z)\,|u(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})|^{2}

\displaystyle+\int(\partial^{4}a)(y-y^{\prime})(h\cdot|u|^{2})(y,z)\,|u(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})|^{2}.

Substituting (
A.8
) and (
A.9
) into (
A.6
) and integrating over
[0,T]
, we finally obtain the following theorem.

Theorem A.1
.

If
u
is the solution of (
QNLS
) and the metric
g
satisfies the smallness condition
\|g-I\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\ll 1
and the evolution equation

\partial_{t}g^{\alpha\beta}=H^{\alpha\beta},

(A.10)

then the following estimate holds:

\displaystyle\|u\|_{L^{4}_{t,y}L^{2}_{z}}^{4}\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}^{3}\|\partial_{x}u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+\sum_{j=1}^{4}\|\mathrm{Err}_{i}\|_{L^{1}_{t}},

(A.11)

where

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{Err}_{1,2}\|_{L^{1}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|\partial_{x}u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|\operatorname{Im}(\bar{u}\mathcal{N}(u))\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}_{x}}

(A.12)

\displaystyle\quad+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}(\|H\cdot u\cdot\partial_{x}u,\ \partial_{x}h\cdot((\partial_{x}u)^{2}+u\cdot\partial_{x}^{2}u)\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}),

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int a_{k}(y-y^{\prime})\,|u(y,z)|^{2}\,\operatorname{Re}\big(\mathcal{N}(u)\partial^{k}\overline{u}-\bar{u}\partial^{k}\mathcal{N}(u)\big)(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{Err}_{3,4}\|_{L^{1}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big|\int(|\nabla_{y}|^{-2}\partial_{y}|u(y,z)|^{2}\cdot(\partial_{x}h\cdot|u|^{2})(y,z^{\prime})\,\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\int|\nabla_{y}|^{-2}|u(y,z)|^{2}\cdot(\partial_{x}h\cdot\partial_{x}|u|^{2})(y,z^{\prime})\,\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\int(|\nabla_{y}|^{-1}|u(y,z)|^{2}\cdot(\partial_{x}h\cdot|u|^{2})(y,z^{\prime})\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\int R^{4}_{y}|u(y,z)|^{2}\cdot(h\cdot|u|^{2})(y,z^{\prime}))\Big|.
